File-backed read of the committed `comms/gpu_spend_ledger.json` (the burst workflow's spend-gate + append target); the sibling of `/insights/usage` for GPU spend. Best-effort: missing/garbled ledger → zeroed envelope, never a 5xx. Surfaced on the dashboard GPU-spend panel. Tier 1 | `comms/gpu_spend_ledger.json` via `src.runtime.gpu_spend.summarize_spend` (`src/web/api/routers/gpu_spend.py`) |
| `GET /api/pnl/history?days=N&account_id=X` | `PnlHistoryPoint[]` (S-063) — `account_id=X` scopes to one account (default: all real-money accounts; **paper excluded** via the `account_class`-aware predicate, falling back to `is_demo` for un-backfilled rows). | `trade_journal.db` (closed trades, realised PnL per UTC day) |
| `GET /api/pnl` | `{schema_version, accounts:{<account_id>:{realized_usd, unrealized_usd, trades_today}}, as_of_utc}` (S-013 M2) — per-account realised/unrealised P&L. **SESSION-GATED** (`Depends(require_session)`) — one of exactly TWO enforced routes; see the `POST /api/auth/login` row. The account roster comes from `config/accounts.yaml`, so an account with **zero** trades still appears with all-zero values (a present-but-zero row is "no trades", not "account missing"); a DB `account_id` absent from the YAML is surfaced too, so a legacy id is never silently dropped. Real-money only — the same canonical `not_paper` + `exclude_reconciler` + `exclude_superseded` predicates the other aggregates use. `trades_today` counts UTC-today opens and **excludes refusal statuses** (`rejected`/`exchange_rejected`/`rejected_too_small`/`orphaned`), so it reflects real exchange submissions, not signal volume. Missing DB file → all zeros (200); a `sqlite3.Error` → 503 `pnl_unavailable`. Distinct from the **no-session** `/api/pnl/history` above, which is what both consumers actually call. | `trade_journal.db::trades` + `config/accounts.yaml` (`src/web/api/routers/pnl.py`) |
| `GET /api/status` | the raw `runtime_status.json` payload the pipeline tick writes, served verbatim. **SESSION-GATED** (`Depends(require_session)`) — the second of the two enforced routes. Never reads the live bot process; it only tails the file the tick loop writes, so a stale file means a stalled tick (`heartbeat.txt` is the canonical liveness signal — see § "Important Notes"). Absent file → 503 `status_unavailable` (`no status file yet`); unreadable/garbled → 503 `status_unavailable` (`status file unreadable`) — the two reasons are deliberately distinct, so "never started" is never confused with "corrupted". | `runtime_logs/runtime_status.json` via `src/web/runtime_status.py::STATUS_PATH` (`src/web/api/routers/status.py`) |
| `GET /api/health` | `{"ok": true}` — a fixed-form **unauthenticated liveness probe**. Defined directly on the app in `src/web/api/main.py` (NOT a router module), which is why it does not appear in `src/web/api/routers/`. It proves the FastAPI process is up and serving; it asserts **nothing** about the trader, the DB, or the mirror — a green `/api/health` beside a stale heartbeat is a normal, meaningful state (that is exactly the split `ict-web-api-recover` vs `ict-liveness-watchdog` exists to service). Tier 1 | none — constant response (`src/web/api/main.py`) |
| `GET /api/bot/pnl/exchange?days=N` | `{summary:{fill_count, total_fees, symbol_count, window_days, total_realized_pnl, total_unrealized_pnl}, by_symbol:[{symbol, fill_count, gross_qty, gross_notional, total_fees, first_exec_time, last_exec_time, realized_pnl, unrealized_pnl, open_qty_signed, last_price}]}` — **exchange-truth P&L attribution** from the exchange-fills store (FIFO buy/sell lot pairing; realised = matched-lot PnL − fees, unrealised marks open lots against the last fill price). `days` 1..90 (default 7). Insulated from `trade_journal.db` schema bugs; zero aggregates when the fills puller has never run. Tier 1 | `runtime_state/exchange_fills.sqlite` (populated by `scripts/pull_exchange_fills.py`) via `src.runtime.exchange_fills_store` (`src/web/api/routers/pnl_exchange.py`) |
| `GET /api/bot/pnl/exchange/fills?days=N&account_id=X&symbol=X&limit=N` | `{fills:[{exec_id, account_id, symbol, side, price, qty, fee, fee_currency, exec_time, order_id, is_maker}], count, days, account_id, symbol, limit, truncated}` — the individual exchange fill **ROWS**, newest-first. Every sibling on this router returns a SUM, which is the right default and cannot answer *which trade* a discrepancy belongs to when several strategies trade one symbol on one account. Measured 2026-08-07: the journal recorded −$9,669.41 across three AVAXUSDT/`bybit_1` trades where exchange truth was −$5,403.09, a **$4,266.32** gap that no existing surface could attribute over a population of **six fills** (`BL-20260807-EXCHANGE-TRUTH-PER-STRATEGY-UNREACHABLE`). **Attributes nothing, deliberately** — a broker SL/TP exit fills under an order id the bot never sees (`src/runtime/broker_cost_attribution.py`), so an `order_id → strategy` map covers ENTRIES only and a per-strategy aggregate built on it would bucket every exit as `unattributed` and still print a confident split. Rows are the substrate an attributor is CHECKED against. `symbol` is the exact stored **venue** form (`AVAX/USDT:USDT`, not `AVAXUSDT`) and is bound, never interpolated; `limit` 1..1000. **`truncated`** declares a page that hit the cap, so a short list is never mistaken for a complete population. Tier 1 | `runtime_state/exchange_fills.sqlite` via `src.runtime.exchange_fills_store::list_fills` (`src/web/api/routers/pnl_exchange.py`) |
| `GET /api/bot/pnl/broker-truth?account_id=X` | `{present, count, account_id, accounts:[{account_id, realized_usd, fees_usd, funding_usd, as_of, window_start, window_end, source, sub_accounts, note}], updated_at}` — **authoritative per-account lifetime realized PnL (broker wallet-truth)** for accounts whose live per-row journal `pnl` can't be trusted (netting + spot/perp + a sub-account switch ⇒ the journal under-records; `bybit_2` = **−$262.52** wallet-truth vs ~−$33 journal — BL-20260713-BYBIT2-PNL-UNDERRECORD). File-backed read of the committed `comms/broker_truth_ledger.json` (written by a reviewed `scripts/ops/reconcile_netting_pnl.py --emit-ledger` run from an operator's Bybit UM export). **NOT a money-DB rewrite** — records the account-level truth next to the journal's approximate figure; per-row `pnl` is left unmodified. `account_id=X` filters to one account. The sibling of `/api/bot/gpu/spend`. Best-effort: missing/garbled ledger → `present:false`, never a 5xx. Tier 1 | `comms/broker_truth_ledger.json` via `src.runtime.broker_truth.summarize_broker_truth` (`src/web/api/routers/pnl_broker_truth.py`) |
| `GET /api/bot/news/recent?limit=N` | `{present, log_path, count, records[]}` — newest-first tail of the M9 news layer's shadow-soak log (per-actionable-signal decision/adjustment/veto/query/symbol + applied influence downsizes). Each record also carries **`top_items`** — the up-to-3 highest-\|score\| news articles the layer actually read, as `{headline, url, score}` (added 2026-07-06 from `NewsScoreResult.raw_scores`) — so a consumer can render a clickable ticker linking to the source articles that drove the decision (empty list when the layer read nothing scoreable). `present:false` until the layer is active (`NEWS_SOURCE=rss`, or `newsapi` + `NEWS_API_KEY`). Tier 1 | `runtime_logs/news_decisions.jsonl` (written by `src.news.news_audit` + `src.runtime.news_sizing`) |
| `GET /api/bot/exit-ladder/soak?limit=N&venue=api\|prop&account_id=X&differing=BOOL` | `{present, log_path, count, records[], summary{total_scanned, by_venue, differing, differing_pct}}` — newest-first tail of the **ExitPlan exit-ladder shadow-soak** (dynamic-take-profit consistency P3). One row per executed order: the **laddered exit that would be used** (materialized ExitPlan sized to the order's real qty) vs the **single SL/TP target actually placed** (`differs_from_single_target`). Observe-only — nothing reads it back to drive an exit (graduation to the real laddered exit is the backtest-gated P4). `present:false` until the first live opening order writes a row. Tier 1 | `runtime_logs/exit_ladder_soak.jsonl` (written by `src.runtime.exit_ladder_soak` from `execute.py` (venue=api) + `breakout_executor.py` (venue=prop)) |
| `GET /api/bot/fc-geometry/soak?limit=N&symbol=X&account_id=X&fc_only=BOOL` | `{present, log_path, count, records[], summary{total_scanned, by_symbol, fc_present, fc_coverage_pct}}` — newest-first tail of the **fc-geometry shadow-soak** (M19 D1). One row per live opening order: the SL/TP **actually placed** next to the decision-time **quantile-forecast snapshot** (`fc_*` row from `forecast_live`, `fc_present:false` logged when no forecast was served — the honest coverage denominator). The live writer logs decision-time facts only; the fc-scaled counterfactual + **censoring-aware** outcome resolution happen trainer-side (`scripts/ml/fc_geometry_resolve.py`) so the scaling parameterization is never baked into the soak. Observe-only — nothing reads it back; any fc→geometry order change is Tier-3 (`MB-20260705-FC-SLTP-GEOMETRY`). `present:false` until the first live opening order writes a row. Tier 1 | `runtime_logs/fc_geometry_soak.jsonl` (written by `src.runtime.fc_geometry_soak` from `execute.py`) |
| `GET /api/bot/allocator/soak?limit=N&symbol=X&regret=BOOL` | `{present, log_path, count, records[], summary{total_scanned, disagree, disagree_pct, mean_regret}}` — newest-first tail of the **portfolio capital-allocator shadow-soak** (M18 P0c). One row per tick with **≥ 2 actionable candidates** (a genuine choice): what a capital allocator **would** pick (the top-ranked candidate of the full opportunity set the intent multiplexer surfaces, M18 P0b) vs what the aggregator **actually** routed, + the **regret** (`top_score − executed_score`, ≥ 0 — EV left on the table). The ranking `score_kind` is **`ev_net_r`** — the cost-aware EV scorer (`src/runtime/allocator_ev.py::candidate_ev_score`, M18 P1, #5098) replaced the P0c confidence proxy. NOTE the 2026-06-30 backtest findings (`docs/research/M18-allocator-backtest-findings-2026-06-29.md`): the EV scorer's SELECTION does not beat dumb priority (sizing-normalized edge −$7; ranker OOS AUC ≈ 0.51) — P2/P3 (allocator selects) are PARKED pending a proven P_win input. `regret=true` filters to disagreements. Observe-only — routing is unchanged; nothing reads it back (graduating the allocator to *select* the subset is the backtest-gated M18 P2+). `present:false` until the first multi-candidate tick writes a row. Tier 1 | `runtime_logs/allocator_soak.jsonl` (written by `src.runtime.allocator_soak` from `intent_multiplexer.py`) |
| `GET /api/bot/exposure/soak?limit=N&account_id=X&measured_only=BOOL` | `{present, log_path, count, records[], summary{total_scanned, by_account{<id>:{rows, measured_n, unmeasured_n, max_multiple, min_multiple, last_multiple, last_logged_at_utc, policy_declared}}, venue_sessions{}}}` — newest-first tail of the **gross-exposure observation soak** (2026-08-09). One row per declared account per cadence window (`EXPOSURE_SOAK_SECONDS`, default 900s), carrying the account's `report()["exposure"]` **verbatim and uncollapsed** — `measured` / `policy_declared` / `exposure_multiple` (null, **never `0.0`**, when unmeasured: *"we could not look"* and *"the account is flat"* are opposite statements and a fabricated zero would drag a per-account min to a value never observed). **Exists because the ceiling VALUES need a distribution, not a reading**: design § 6 needs the ceiling ABOVE normal operation and BELOW the venue limit, § 7 forbids shipping a value with no soak behind it, and #8665/#8678 made the measurement emittable then readable without ACCUMULATING it — the first read available was a single **Sunday** snapshot of a held book. **`summary.by_account[*].max_multiple` is the load-bearing statistic** (a ceiling clearing the mean but not the peak silently clamps correctly-sized trades at the peak — § 6 calls that worse than no ceiling), and it always ships beside `measured_n`/`rows` so a max is never read over an unstated denominator. Each row also stamps **`venue_session_us_equity`** (`rth`/`extended`/`closed`) so a quiet account that is VENUE-SHUT is distinguishable from one that is REFUSING — indistinguishable from the trades table alone, and the 2026-08-09 near-miss that motivated the field (US holidays are NOT modeled upstream, so `rth` means *scheduled* open, never proof the venue traded). `summary.venue_sessions` shows how much of the window was closed — a soak that is mostly `closed` has not observed normal operation at all. **Observe-only, connection-free** (equity from the balance snapshot, notional from the journal); nothing reads it back and it cannot refuse a trade. Tier 1 | `runtime_logs/exposure_soak.jsonl` (written by `src.runtime.exposure_soak` from the `src/main.py` tick) via `src/web/api/routers/exposure.py` |
| `GET /api/bot/pairs/soak?limit=N&pair=X&event=X` | `{present, log_path, count, records[], summary{total_scanned, by_event}}` — newest-first tail of the **market-neutral pairs-sleeve soak** (M22 D2). One row per configured pair evaluated per closed 1h bar: the spread/z decision + (when the executor acts) the placement/close outcome. `event ∈ {skip_flat, skip_concurrency, skip_size, skip_state_unreadable, half_open, shadow_open, shadow_close, open, open_failed, hold, close}`. **A `skip_state_unreadable` row now also carries `state_read` ∈ {`error`, `absent`}** — `error` = we could not look, `absent` = we looked and open legs carry no package (an anomaly in itself). **Read this beside the `by_event` counts, and check that `close` is non-zero** (2026-08-10, BL-20260810-PAIRS-MAX-HOLD-BARS-NOT-ENFORCED): measured over ALL 2,471 rows the sleeve had **29 `open` and ZERO `close`**, with 958 (38.8%) `skip_state_unreadable`, because `_open_pkg_meta` queried `order_packages` on **`account_id` and `id` — two columns that table does not have** (its PK is the TEXT `order_package_id`, not a rowid alias). The `OperationalError` was swallowed by a broad `except` at DEBUG, so every open pair read as unreadable, the tick skipped, and `max_hold_bars: 20` was **never once evaluated** — `pairs_bnb_btc` legs reached 300–595 bars. Fixed by correcting the query, making the read three-state (`found`/`absent`/`error`, registered with `collapsed-state-guard`), and ALERTING on a persistent unreadable state (rate-limited per pair+reason, since the condition can fire every tick and a per-tick alarm is the desensitized-alarm P1). The tests that should have caught it declared their own `order_packages` with `id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, account_id TEXT` — a schema production does not have — so they passed against a fictional table; they now lift the DDL from `src/units/db/database.py`. **`half_open`** (2026-08-09) is the third leg-state: EXACTLY ONE leg open. `_close_pair` is best-effort per leg and leaves a leg that failed to flatten open, but the tick's predicate asked only "are BOTH legs open?" — so a half-closed pair read as **flat**, the decision saw no position, and the executor was free to open a FRESH pair on top of the stranded leg, stacking a second journal row over one netted exchange position. That is the divergence shape, and nothing else owns the cleanup because the netting-attribution reconciler skips pairs rows by design (`BL-20260808-PAIRS-DIVERGENCE-UNOWNED`). A half-open pair now flattens the stranded leg (live only), alerts (`pairs_half_open` — CRITICAL while the leg still stands, WARN once flattened: a lone leg is un-hedged directional exposure in a market-neutral sleeve), logs the row with `stranded_legs` + `cleanup_confirmed`, and **places nothing that bar**; `pair` filters to one label (e.g. `SOLUSDT/BTCUSDT`). The pairs sleeve is an **isolated 2-leg order path** (`src.units.strategies.pairs_executor.run_pairs_tick`, once per tick from `src/main.py` — NOT via `multi_account_execute`); a pair at `execution: shadow` (in `config/pairs.yaml`) logs the would-be trade + places nothing, at `execution: live` places both legs on its `account_id` (`bybit_1` demo/paper). **`leg_placement` (added 2026-08-30) is what makes the hedge-mode question GRADEABLE, and it is why `BL-20260830-PAIRS-SOAK-RECORDS-NO-POSITION-IDX-SO-HEDGE-MODE-CANNOT-BE-VERIFIED` was filed**: `OI-20260830-BYBIT-HEDGE-MODE-ARMED-BUT-UNEXERCISED` clears only on a pairs open whose legs carry a hedge `position_idx` AND that faced a concurrent directional position — and **neither fact was recorded anywhere**, so the criterion could not be met however long the sleeve ran (measured: `position_idx` occurred **0 times** across 400 rows, 2026-08-27T05:05:26Z→2026-08-30T19:02:33Z; the leg schema was exactly `{direction, entry_ref, qty, sl, symbol, tp}`). ⚠️ **THE ORDER PATH WAS NEVER BROKEN** — `_place_pair` → `execute_pkg` → `_submit_order` → `apply_position_idx` was always correct; `apply_position_idx` RETURNS the resolved book and the call site discarded it one line later, so the only defect was that the answer was thrown away. One entry per leg, kept **separate from `legs`** (the pure decision's INTENT) because folding a plan into an outcome would make a `shadow_open` leg carry placement fields describing no order. ⚠️ **`position_idx` is what we SENT, never a venue read-back** — the venue's own view is `/api/diag/bybit_open_orders`, an independent cross-check, and reading one as the other is the semantic substitution this field exists to end. `position_idx_state` carries the resolver's own four states because a bare `null` idx cannot separate `one_way` (an absent kwarg is CORRECT) from `unresolved` (hedge declared, book unreadable, so Bybit REFUSES the order) — opposite outcomes. **`directional_open`** ∈ `present`/`absent`/**`unreadable`** (*we could not look*, never `absent`) says whether a NON-PAIRS position was open on that symbol when the leg was placed: under one-way netting a pairs leg only strands when there is a directional book to net against, so a pair that opened cleanly with `absent` on both legs **was never at risk and proves nothing about hedge mode**. Observe-only surface — nothing reads it back. `present:false` until the first bar writes a row. Tier 1 | `runtime_logs/pairs_soak.jsonl` (written by `src.runtime.pairs_soak` from `pairs_executor.py`) |
| `POST /api/bot/prop/report` | ingest the **inbound prop fill/close or account-status report-back** (Breakout manual-bridge P2). Body is JSON: a **fill/close** (`account_id, symbol, direction, status∈{placed,open,filled,closed,skipped}, entry_price, exit_price, qty, pnl, pnl_percent, reason, ticket_id?`) or an **account status** (`kind:"account_status", account_id, balance, equity, realized_today, unrealized, day_start_balance?`). **`status:"placed"`** is the working-order state — a limit/pending order placed but NOT filled yet (no position, no P&L): it advances the ticket to `placed` (distinct from `filled`) and fires **no** notification; when the limit later trips the operator reports `open`/`filled`. Lifecycle `emitted → [placed] → filled → closed`. A `status:"closed"` fill fires the `prop_closed` notification (the trade-close follow-up); `open`/`filled` fire `prop_fill`. Writes the prop journal, links the fill to its outbound ticket, advances the ticket lifecycle. **Tier 2** (DB write + notification); **token-gated via `DASHBOARD_API_TOKEN`, FAIL-CLOSED** — `_require_write_token` refuses with **503** when the var is unset rather than accepting an anonymous write, so a dropped `.env` value (e.g. a VM migration) can never reopen an anonymous write hole (BL-20260705-DASHBOARD-API-TOKEN-UNSET); missing / wrong-scheme / wrong bearer → 401. *(Corrected 2026-08-09: this row read "token-gated … **when set**", describing the permissive `_check_admin_token` shape that the prop write path deliberately does NOT use — the `devices` routes do. Field beats comment; the gate is authoritative.)* 400 on a structurally-bad report. | `trade_journal.db::{prop_fills,prop_account_status,prop_tickets}` via `src.prop.prop_report.ingest_report` |
| `GET /api/bot/prop/fills?account_id=X&limit=N` | `{present, count, fills[]}` — inbound prop fill/close reports, newest-first | `trade_journal.db::prop_fills` |
| `GET /api/bot/prop/tickets?account_id=X&status=Y&limit=N` | `{present, count, tickets[]}` — OUTBOUND prop tickets the bot emitted (`emitted`/`placed`/`filled`/`closed`/`skipped`; `placed` = limit order placed but not yet filled), newest-first | `trade_journal.db::prop_tickets` (written by `breakout_executor.emit_prop_ticket`) |
| `GET /api/bot/prop/status?account_id=X` | `{account_id, present, status, status_age_hours, status_freshness, rule_distance}` — latest account-status snapshot + **computed rule-distance**: distance to the daily-loss limit ($150 = 3% on the $5k account) and the static-DD floor ($300 / $4700 floor), resolved from the account's prop ruleset. Drives the dashboard rule-distance panel. Null any value not derivable from the snapshot. ⚠️ **READ `status_freshness` BEFORE TREATING ANY DISTANCE AS A LIVE CUSHION** (added 2026-08-14). Every number here is a function of ONE operator-reported row and the manual bridge has no broker feed to refresh it, so a three-week-old snapshot renders a full-looking cushion to an account-killer that is **indistinguishable from a live one** — and `present` was the only field a consumer had, which says a row EXISTS, never that it is CURRENT. Four states, never collapsed, sharing `prop_balance`'s vocabulary: `ok` (inside `PROP_STATUS_REQUEST_MAX_AGE_HOURS`) · `stale` (older than it, **or undateable** — a snapshot that cannot be dated cannot be shown to be current, and the fail-safe reading of a safety cushion is stale, matching `prop_balance.prop_sizing_balance`'s refusal on the same row) · `absent` (nothing ever reported) · `unchecked` (the threshold is `<= 0`, so the check is off — *we did not look*, which is **not** `ok`). A fifth value, **`error`**, appears only on the envelope when the read itself failed (we never reached a snapshot to grade); `rule_distance` is then `null`. `status_age_hours` is `None` for BOTH absent and undateable — read `status_freshness` to tell them apart, never the null. The distances are still returned when stale (discarding the last known cushion helps nobody); the caveat travels **inside** `rule_distance` as well as on the envelope, so a consumer reading only the panel cannot miss it. Tier 1 | `trade_journal.db::prop_account_status` + `config/prop_rulesets/breakout.yaml` |
| `GET /api/bot/prop/reconcile?account_id=X` | `{present, summary{tickets_total, fills_total, unacted_count}, unacted_tickets[]}` — **un-acted tickets** (emitted, past `valid_until`, no matching fill reported back) — the P3 drift alert. Tier 1 | `trade_journal.db::{prop_tickets,prop_fills}` via `src.prop.prop_reconcile` |
| `GET /api/bot/reports?limit=N&window=X` | `{present, count, total, window, reports:[{id, window, generated_at, window_start, window_end, roll_up_grade, headline, html_path, json_path, md_path}, ...]}` — newest-first index of consolidated **system reports** (the `/system-report` master skill's output). `window ∈ {since-last,daily,weekly,monthly}` filters. **File-backed, Tier 1, read-only** (no DB table). Surfaced as a "Reports" log of links in the Svelte SPA. | `comms/reports/index.json` (committed; VM mirrors via `ict-git-sync`) via `src/web/api/routers/reports.py` |
| `GET /api/bot/reports/{report_id}` | `{present, report, html, json_present}` — one report's index metadata + its rendered self-contained responsive `report.html` body (for inline embed / WebView). 404 on unknown id; artifact paths validated under `comms/reports/` (no traversal). Tier 1 | `comms/reports/<window>/<ts>/report.html` |
| `GET /api/bot/notifications` | `{generated_at, count, has_alerts, banners:[{severity∈alert\|warning\|info, kind∈trainer_down\|account_down\|operator_warning\|orphan_unreconciled\|prop_fills_stale\|trade_open, message, detail, since}]}` — the **banner feed** the SPA renders at the top of Overview for can't-miss conditions (NOT routine pings): **trainer_down** (the trainer VM's 2-min mirror heartbeat stale ⇒ ML training stalled; from `trainer_reachability_alert.status()`), **account_down** (one per latched-down live broker account, from `account_reachability_alert.down_accounts()`), **operator_warning** (the last `NOTIF_WARNING_WINDOW_MIN`m of persisted `outcomes.jsonl` WARN+/ERROR/CRITICAL — the same feed that Telegrams ERROR/CRITICAL — so a live operational condition like a stuck position-close surfaces on the banner, not only in Telegram; CRITICAL/ERROR→`alert`, **WARN**→`warning`; deduped digit-normalized so a per-tick repeat collapses to one, newest-first, capped at 3. ⚠️ **The level string is `warn`, NOT `warning`** — `src/runtime/outcomes.py`'s `Level` enum has exactly four members, `info`/`warn`/`error`/`critical`, and `emit()` persists `level.value`. This row previously said "WARNING→`warning`", and so did the filter: `notifications.py::_WARN_LEVELS` was `{"WARNING","ERROR","CRITICAL"}`, which `"warn".upper() == "WARN"` never matched — so **every** `Level.WARN` outcome was persisted by `_PERSIST_LEVELS` and then silently dropped by the only consumer meant to surface it, while ERROR/CRITICAL kept working and made the banner look healthy. Fixed 2026-08-13 by accepting `WARN` (`BL-20260813-OPERATOR-WARNING-BANNER-CANNOT-MATCH-WARN`); do not "tidy" the set back to the doc's old spelling — match the value the producer writes), **orphan_unreconciled** (real-money `status='orphaned'` rows still awaiting reconciliation and not yet investigated-terminal — orphaned rows appear in NEITHER Positions nor Trades, so without this banner they are invisible to both apps; added 2026-07-13, BL-20260713-BYBIT2-BTC-ORPHANS-UNRECONCILED), **prop_fills_stale** (the prop journal is missing trades the venue already took — a balance that moved between two operator reports with no fills reported in between, or a bracket already announced as crossed whose position is still open; from `prop_fills_staleness.stale_fill_accounts()`, added 2026-08-23. ⚠️ It reports **unrecorded closes, never unacted tickets** — an unanswered ticket is the expected shape on a manual bridge and a banner for it would be the desensitized-alarm P1), and **trade_open** (best-effort compact "N trades opened in the last `TRADE_OPEN_BANNER_WINDOW_MIN`m" info line). Banners sorted alert-first. **Connection-free, best-effort** (any source failure omits that kind, never 5xxs). Tier 1 (operator-requested 2026-07-08). | `src/runtime/trainer_reachability_alert.py` + `src/runtime/account_reachability_alert.py` + `runtime_logs/outcomes.jsonl` + `trade_journal.db::trades` (`src/web/api/routers/notifications.py`) |
| `GET /api/bot/roadmap` | `{present, lastUpdated, summary{done,active,pending,total}, milestones:[{id, type, focus, status, statusEmoji, statusLabel, statusDetail, sprintRefs, sprintIds, sprintCount}], sprints:[{id, title, objective, dateStart, dateEnd, milestone, path}], sprintCount}` — the parsed **product roadmap**: the `## Milestone Roadmap` table (each milestone with a normalized `status` ∈ done/in_progress/next/planned/blocked/reopened + a progress roll-up), plus the full **sprint-log index** (every `docs/sprint-logs/*.md`, newest-first by end date). Each sprint is mapped to its owning milestone via the explicit `docs/sprint-logs/<id>.md` links in the milestone cell + the Historical Sprint Ledger's `M-mapping` column + a filename-prefix fallback; one-off thematic sprints resolve `milestone:null`. **File-backed, Tier 1, read-only** (no DB table) — backs the SPA's **Roadmap** tab. Best-effort: a missing/garbled `ROADMAP.md` degrades to an empty envelope, never a 5xx. Short in-process cache keyed on file mtimes. | `ROADMAP.md` + `docs/sprint-logs/*.md` via `src/web/api/routers/roadmap.py` |
| `GET /api/bot/roadmap/sprint/{sprint_id}` | `{present, id, path, milestone, dateStart, dateEnd, objective, sections:[{heading, body}], markdown}` — one sprint log parsed into its `##` sections plus the raw markdown (the work-session write-up: objective, work completed, validation, docs updated, …). `present:false` (HTTP 200) on unknown id; `sprint_id` validated `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+` and resolved strictly under `docs/sprint-logs/` (no traversal → 400). Tier 1 | `docs/sprint-logs/<sprint_id>.md` |
| `GET /api/bot/work` | `{present, reason, intents, objects, steps, readErrors, lifecycle{dormant,ready,in_flight,waiting,done,accepted,unknown}, summary{intentCount,objectCount,stepCount,objectsGraded,readErrorCount}, wip{ceiling,inFlight,enforced,state,note}, coverage{complete,scope,carriedRowsApprox,carriedRowsMigrateIn,note}}` — the **work store** (F1 of the operating model): intents → objects → steps, with a lifecycle roll-up and each object's typed `blocked_on` edges. Backs the SPA's Work section. **File-backed, Tier 1, read-only** (no DB table), cached on file mtimes, degrading to an empty envelope rather than a 5xx — the `/api/bot/roadmap` shape over a different directory. ⚠️ **`coverage.complete` is `false` on EVERY response, including the degraded envelope, and it is not decoration**: a consumer rendering this store as the whole of system work is wrong — there are no `steps`, and no audit has established that every workstream has an object. ⚠️ **This sentence read "the store holds the operating-layer build's own 8 phases and nothing else … the ~572 carried backlog rows migrate in Phase C" until 2026-09-01, and BOTH halves are now false**: Phase C merged and the store holds **584 objects**, of which (measured 2026-09-01) **577 are `dormant`, 3 `in_flight`, 4 `waiting`**. Read the LIFECYCLE, not the count — 584 objects is not 584 things in flight. ⚠️ **`lifecycle` is never collapsed**: all six declared states ship as explicit keys with explicit zeros, plus **`unknown`** for a row that could not be graded — and the buckets **sum to the object count by construction**, so the partition is checkable rather than trusted. ⚠️ **A file that fails to parse is REPORTED in `readErrors` and counted in `lifecycle.unknown`, never dropped** — otherwise *we could not read the store* and *the store is empty* render identically (the `silent-empty-guard` shape, consumer side). ⚠️ **`blockedOnState` separates `declared_none` from `unstated`**: an empty `blocked_on` is a CLAIM that nothing blocks the object (the work store's README is explicit on this), whereas an absent key means nobody has said. Reading the second as the first is how a false *ready* appears. `refResolvedInStore` is graded **only** for a `kind: object` edge and is `null` otherwise — an `external_event` edge names something outside the store and is not a dangling reference. ⚠️ **`wip.enforced` is `true` and `wip.state` says so as of 2026-09-01** — `scripts/ci/check_wip_ceiling.py` FAILS CI on a ninth `in_flight` object and an exception needs an approved `docs/claude/work/wip-ceiling-exception.yaml`. ⚠️ **This row said `false` / `declared_not_enforced` until 2026-09-01 and that was stale in the DANGEROUS direction** — it told a reader a limit binding their CI was advisory, the same drift the route's own comment block records the SPA rendering to the operator's screenshot. ⚠️ **Enforcement is in CI, NOT in this route**: the count here is a reading and the route gates nothing; a read path that refused something would be a second, drifting copy of the rule. Tier 1 | `docs/claude/work/{intents,objects,steps}/*.yaml` via `src/web/api/routers/work.py` |
| `GET /api/bot/work/object/{object_id}` | `{present, id, object}` — one work object in full, including its free-form keys (`scope_split`, `carried_out_of_scope`, the emoji-prefixed warning keys) preserved verbatim under `object.extra`, because dropping them would hide exactly the caveats they exist to raise. `present:false` (HTTP 200) on an unknown id. ⚠️ **A file that EXISTS but cannot be parsed returns `present:false` WITH an `error`** — *found-but-unreadable* must not read as *no such object*. `object_id` is validated `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$` (anchored to a leading alphanumeric so `..` is refused at the door) and resolved strictly under `docs/claude/work/objects/` → 400. Tier 1 | `docs/claude/work/objects/<object_id>.yaml` |
| `GET /api/bot/work/decisions` | `{present, requests[], unanswerableOperatorEdges[], summary{awaitingOperator, awaitingCommit, decided, byAnswerState, requestCount, malformedRequestsDropped, unanswerableOperatorEdgeCount, staleOpenWindows, staleAfterSeconds}, transit{state, error, path, rowsRead}, writeGate{state, acceptsWrites, note}}` — **the decision inbox** (Phase H, the control half). Every decision the store is waiting on the operator for, with its options, so the SPA can render *anything waiting on the operator, at the top* and let them answer it. ⚠️ **`answerState` is FOUR states, never collapsed** — `not_submitted` · `in_transit` · `committed` · `unreadable` — and **`committed` is graded from the `answer` block on the work object IN THE REPO, never from the transit log**. That is the whole transit contract, not an implementation detail: **transit fails BACK, never forward**, so an answer that does not reach the repo leaves its question **unanswered** rather than ambiguous. A question wrongly shown as answered is a decision nobody made. ⚠️ **`unreadable` is *we could not look*** — collapsing it into `not_submitted` would report a broken channel as *the operator has not answered*, putting a question back on the operator they may already have answered and making a broken channel indistinguishable from a quiet one. Registered with `collapsed-state-guard` as `work_decisions.answer_state`. ⚠️ **`awaitingOperator` and `awaitingCommit` are DIFFERENT populations and are deliberately not pooled**: an `in_transit` question is also unanswered, but it is waiting on a COMMITTER, and counting it as the operator's would put work on their plate that is not theirs. ⚠️ **`unanswerableOperatorEdges` is the gap worth reading** — a `blocked_on` edge of `kind: operator_decision` that carries NO answerable request, i.e. a question the operator is blocking on and *cannot answer from the UI because nobody wrote it down*. ⚠️ **`transit.state` separates `absent` from `unreadable`**: an append-only log never written genuinely holds nothing; one we could not open is not a reading. ⚠️ **`writeGate.state` is published on the READ route deliberately** (`open` / `closed_no_token` / `unknown`) so the SPA can say *answering is closed* instead of rendering a submit button that 503s — the token's VALUE is never echoed, only whether one is set. **Measured 2026-09-01 before this shipped: of 584 objects, ZERO carried a `decision_requests` block and ZERO carried an `operator_decision` edge** — so the inbox was empty by construction, and `WO-20260901-PHASE-H` carries the first real question as its own content (the Phase A precedent). Tier 1 | `docs/claude/work/objects/*.yaml` + `runtime_logs/work_decision_transit.jsonl` via `src/runtime/work_decisions.py` |
| `POST /api/bot/work/decision` | `{accepted, submissionId, objectId, requestId, submittedAt, answerState, note}` — submit ONE answer to a decision request. Body `{object_id, request_id, chosen?, free_text?}`. **Tier 2** (operator write). ⚠️ **Token-gated via `DASHBOARD_API_TOKEN`, FAIL-CLOSED** — **503** when the var is unset rather than accepting an anonymous write, copying `POST /api/bot/prop/report`'s polarity and deliberately NOT the permissive `devices` shape nor `learning/progress`'s unauthenticated one: an anonymous write here would let anyone on the internet answer a question in the operator's name on a public, browser-reachable host. Missing / wrong-scheme / wrong bearer → 401. ⚠️ **IT DECIDES NOTHING.** It appends one submission to the live layer's transit log and writes **no truth** — the response comes back `answerState: in_transit`, **never `committed`**, and the question stays unanswered until `scripts/ops/commit_work_decisions.py` (or a session) writes the answer into the object YAML in the repo. Refuses: a `chosen` naming an option the author never declared (400) · an empty submission with neither a choice nor free text (400, the same rule `board-post.yml` applies to a blank comment — a vacuous answer that reads as compliance is worse than none) · a traversing `object_id` (400) · an unknown object or request (404) · **a request that already carries an `answer` (409 — a committed decision is changed by editing the repo, never by queueing a second answer behind one that landed)**. A write that fails to persist is a **503, never a 200**: a submission the operator believes landed and did not is the forward failure the contract refuses. | `runtime_logs/work_decision_transit.jsonl` via `src.runtime.work_decisions.append_submission` |
| `GET /api/bot/learning/curriculum` | `{present, curriculum}` — the committed **learning curriculum** (tracks → modules → resources) served for the dashboard **Learning** tab. Best-effort: `present:false` on a missing/garbled file (the dashboard falls back to its bundled copy). File-backed, Tier 1, read-only. Short in-process cache keyed on file mtime. | `comms/learning/curriculum.json` via `src/web/api/routers/learning.py` |
| `GET /api/bot/learning/courses` | `{present, count, courses:[{course_id, title, subtitle, episode_count, quiz_count, has_audio}]}` — index of the **interactive courses** (audio + quiz modules) the SPA's course player renders. Best-effort: `present:false` when the dir is missing. File-backed, Tier 1, read-only. | `comms/learning/courses/*.json` via `src/web/api/routers/learning.py` |
| `GET /api/bot/learning/courses/{course_id}` | `{present, course}` — one course: `{course_id, title, subtitle, hosts, episodes:[{id, title, summary, script?, audio?, drive_id?, audio_url?}], quiz:[{id,q,options,answer,explain}]}`. An episode's audio is committed in-repo (`audio`), **Google-Drive-hosted** (`drive_id` — the standard for large audio; the shared learning Drive folder is the content store), or a hosted URL (`audio_url`); script-only episodes play via the client's built-in TTS. `present:false` (HTTP 200) on unknown id; `course_id` validated `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*` and resolved strictly under `comms/learning/courses/` (no traversal → 400). Tier 1 | `comms/learning/courses/<course_id>.json` |
| `GET /api/bot/learning/progress` | `{present, items:{<resource_id>:{status,note,updated_at}}, summary:{tracked,done,in_progress}}` — the operator's per-resource learning progress (durable + cross-device). Best-effort degraded envelope (never a 5xx) on a DB error. Tier 1 | `trade_journal.db::learning_progress` |
| `POST /api/bot/learning/progress` | upsert one resource's progress — body `{resource_id, status∈{not_started,in_progress,done}, note?}` → the stored row. **Unauthenticated** client self-service write (same shape as `POST /devices/register`), so the SPA can record progress without holding `DASHBOARD_API_TOKEN`; 400 on a bad status / missing id. **Tier 1** (operator observability — no trading impact, no order path, no notification; the store holds no secret). | `trade_journal.db::learning_progress` via `src/web/api/routers/learning.py` |
| `POST /api/bot/devices/register` | upsert a device by its FCM token (M12 S1) — `{token, platform?∈{android,ios}, label?, subscriptions?}` → `{id, token_suffix, platform, label, subscriptions, created_at, last_seen_at, is_new}`. **Idempotent on token** (re-register updates `last_seen_at`/label/subscriptions, no dup row). The raw token is never echoed back (only `token_suffix` = last 8 chars). Unknown subscription kinds (vs `src.runtime.mobile_push.event_kinds`) → 400. ⚠️ **The only write the Android app made to the bot — retired from the live feed 2026-09-01** (`device_tokens` is empty on the live host: 0 registered devices). Tier 1 | `trade_journal.db::device_tokens` (`src/web/api/routers/devices.py`) |
| `GET /api/bot/devices` | `{count, devices:[{id, token_suffix, platform, label, subscriptions, created_at, last_seen_at}]}` — registered devices (raw tokens never exposed). **Token-gated via `DASHBOARD_API_TOKEN`** when set (serves permissively when unset). Tier 1 | `trade_journal.db::device_tokens` |
| `GET /api/bot/devices/event-kinds` | `{kinds:[{kind, label, description, in_flight}]}` — the canonical push event-kind taxonomy the (retired 2026-09-01) Android Notifications screen iterated to build per-kind toggles (so the app needn't mirror the list). Tier 1 | `src.runtime.mobile_push.event_kinds` |
| `DELETE /api/bot/devices/{id}` | `{id, deleted:true}` — revoke a device (lost phone). 404 on unknown id. Token-gated via `DASHBOARD_API_TOKEN` when set. Tier 1 | `trade_journal.db::device_tokens` |
| `PATCH /api/bot/devices/{id}/subscriptions` | `{id, subscriptions}` — replace a device's per-kind push subscription prefs (`{subscriptions: null\|[kinds]\|{kind:bool}}`; null/empty = all). 404 on unknown id; unknown kinds → 400. Token-gated when `DASHBOARD_API_TOKEN` set. Tier 1 | `trade_journal.db::device_tokens` |
| `POST /api/auth/login` | `{access_token, token_type, expires_in}` (S-013 M3 PR #1) — issues a bearer session token for the session-gated `/api/pnl` + `/api/status` routes given `{email, password}`. **Enforcement IS live on those two routes** — `auth.require_session` is **default-deny**, with no permissive-when-unconfigured branch: 401 `invalid_session` on a missing/malformed `Authorization`, an expired token, a bad signature, or `alg=none`; 403 `email_not_allowlisted` if the token's `email` claim no longer matches `ALLOWED_EMAIL` (de-allowlisted after issuance); 500 generic `auth_unavailable` (never echoing which var) if the server's own auth env vars are missing, so a dropped secret **fails closed** rather than opening the routes. *(Corrected 2026-08-13: this row read "**Issuance only**: `require_session` enforcement on protected routes is a separate PR" — stale, and stale in the dangerous direction. The dependency is attached on both routes today; field beats comment. Verified by reading `pnl.py` / `status.py` / `auth.py`, not inferred from the merge.)* What remains true is the consumer half: **no consumer has ever called this path** — the retired Streamlit dashboard and Android app consumed only no-session routes, and the Svelte SPA does not call it yet either (it is what Phase H wires up) (`/api/pnl/history`, not `/api/pnl`) — so the gate is invisible to either consumer, and a session-gated route reading 401 from a relay is the CONTRACT, not an outage. `PUBLIC_ROUTES` in `src/web/api/main.py` is the opt-out list, enforced by the test suite. | env-configured allowed email + password hash (`src/web/api/auth.py`) |

### `BotStats` shape
```json
{
  "pnl24h": 124.50,
  "totalPnL": 3200.00,
  "openTrades": 2,
  "winRate": 68.5,
  "status": "running",
  "datasource": "live",
  "vmHealth": { "cpu": 32.1, "memory": 48.5, "disk": 21.0 },
  "pnlCoverage": 0.768,
  "pnlMeasuredCount": 331,
  "pnlEstimatedCount": 15,
  "totalPnLMeasured": -45.63,
  "paperOpenTrades": 1,
  "paper": { "pnl24h": 0.0, "totalPnL": 0.0, "openTrades": 1, "winRate": 0.0,
             "pnlCoverage": 0.0, "pnlMeasuredCount": 0,
             "pnlEstimatedCount": 0, "totalPnLMeasured": 0.0 }
}
```

**`pnlCoverage` / `pnlMeasuredCount` / `pnlEstimatedCount` / `totalPnLMeasured`
(added 2026-08-26, GATE 0 / G3) state how much of `totalPnL` was ever a
MEASUREMENT.** `/stats` is the FIRST number both apps render and it published a
sum and a rate over journal `pnl` with no provenance at all, while
`/api/bot/performance` has carried `pnlCoverage` since 2026-07-31. ⚠️ **The
count and the sum are over DIFFERENT populations, deliberately, and the
definitions are copied from `/performance` rather than re-derived** —
`pnlCoverage`/`pnlMeasuredCount` are MEASURED-only, `totalPnLMeasured` sums
MEASURED+ESTIMATED; two surfaces reporting the same population under the same
key must not disagree, and neither may be harmonised to the other. ⚠️ **`None`
means WE COULD NOT LOOK, never `0.0`** — an older journal with no `notes`
column is ungradeable, and reporting zeros there would assert an observation
nobody made; a MISSING DB file is instead "no trades yet" (the same reading
`_pnl_stats_for` already takes), so its COUNTS are real zeros while the ratio
stays `None`, because a coverage ratio over an empty population does not exist.
The keys are always PRESENT — a key that vanishes makes a consumer branch on
absence, and absence is not one of the states. The block is computed
**outside** the raising path: `_pnl_stats_for` 503s on a broken schema because a
fabricated `pnl24h: 0` reads as "no trades today", but a failed provenance read
leaves the money numbers intact and only the caveat missing, so it degrades to
nulls rather than taking the route down. Measured live 2026-08-26 — population:
closed, non-backtest, `pnl NOT NULL`, real-money, **n=431** — coverage
**0.768**, with `totalPnL` moving **−$23.22 → −$45.63** restricted to
measured+estimated. ⚠️ **That coverage figure is population-specific and must
not be quoted as the journal's**: the same classifier reads **0.425** over all
accounts (n=1,187) and **0.257** over the package-joined population the
2026-08-26 workplan cites as fact 2 (n=806, reproducing its 25.6%). The case
for this block is the SUM nearly doubling, not the coverage being alarming —
and the real-money win rate moves only **0.1pp**, so do not carry the
workplan's "win rate moves 9 points" across to this surface; that contrast is
between the ESTIMATED and MEASURED buckets on a different population.

The top-level `pnl24h` / `totalPnL` / `openTrades` / `winRate` are **real-money
only** — paper rows are excluded (account_class-authoritative, is_demo
fallback). **Real and paper performance are never blended** (P4 of the
live-trade management contract, operator directive): the paper-side aggregates
ride in an **additive `paper` sub-block** (same shape) plus a distinct
`paperOpenTrades` count alongside the real `openTrades` KPI, so a consumer
renders the two as separate sections — there is no combined real+paper total
anywhere.

### `Position` shape (`/api/bot/positions`)
```json
{
  "id": "42",
  "account": "bybit_2",
  "accountClass": "real_money",
  "assetClass": "crypto",
  "symbol": "BTCUSDT",
  "side": "buy",
  "qty": 0.001,
  "entryPrice": 80700,
  "unrealizedPnl": 12.45,
  "openedAt": "2026-05-11T03:00:00Z",
  "stopLoss": 80300,
  "takeProfit": 81450,
  "pattern": "vwap"
}
```

`accountClass` (`"paper"`/`"real_money"`, added 2026-06-15) is the
funding-category axis, orthogonal to the technical `mode:` gate. Never
null — falls back to the legacy `is_demo` boolean for rows predating the
`account_class` column/backfill. `isDemo` is still emitted alongside for
back-compat.

`assetClass` (`"crypto"`/`"index"`/`"commodity"`/`"bond"`/`"equity"`/`"fx"`/
`"unknown"`, added 2026-06-29) is the coarse **reporting** bucket for the row's
`symbol` so a consumer can group/filter positions by asset group without
maintaining a symbol map of its own. Resolved by the same authoritative
`src/web/api/_asset_class.py::asset_class_for_symbol` the `/performance`
`perAssetClass` aggregate uses (config-driven via `config/instruments.yaml`,
heuristic fallback). **Reporting-only** — never the order path; never null
(worst case `"unknown"`). Also emitted on `/api/bot/trades/closed` and
`/api/bot/order-packages` rows.

`stopLoss` / `takeProfit` / `pattern` were added in #820 (2026-05-11) so the
dashboard's live overview chart can render TP/SL price-lines and the
positions table can show the active strategy. Each is **nullable** —
older rows or rows where the writer didn't populate the field serialize
as `null`. Renderers must treat null as "not provided" (em-dash) rather
than `0` / `"unknown"`.

**`r`** (added 2026-08-17, M31 P3) is the **R-geometry of the open trade**, joined from `trade_journal.db::position_telemetry` by trade id: `{openR, peakR, peakState, peakProvenance, peakRIsLowerBound, givebackR, capR, pctOfCap, peakPctOfCap, rToStop, rToTarget, rrFromHere, barsHeld, barsSincePeak, armR, armReach, updatedAt}`. **`null` when the leg writes no telemetry row** — only the donchian/pullback monitors do — which is the honest value: absent, never a zeroed block (zeros would assert a measured flat trade). `rrFromHere = rToTarget / rToStop` is upside left against give-back at risk, the field the *"should we hold this?"* question needs; on the motivating XRP trade it is **0.71**, i.e. holding for the target risks more than it stands to make. ⚠️ **`peakR` is a LOWER BOUND**, hence `peakRIsLowerBound` shipping beside it. One telemetry read per RESPONSE (not per row — an N+1 on the blocking sqlite path is what the route's `asyncio.to_thread` offload exists to prevent), and best-effort: a telemetry outage degrades a position to `r: null` rather than breaking the route.

`options` (added 2026-06-27, Alpaca options Slice-5) is the **defined-risk
structure block** for an options-expression row (account_class `paper`,
`alpaca_options_paper`): `{structure:"debit_vertical", contracts, net_debit,
max_loss_usd, max_gain_usd, width, breakeven, expiration, legs:[{symbol(OCC),
side, intent, ratio, strike, type}]}`. **`null` for any non-options row.**
Decision-time geometry persisted in the trade's `notes.options` at open by
`execute._log_trade_to_journal` (via `options_overlay.options_structure_dict`)
and surfaced here connection-free — **per-leg live greeks/PnL are a documented
follow-up** (the `/positions` endpoint never opens a broker socket). The
options-lifecycle reconciler (`order_monitor._reconcile_options_expiry_and_assignment`)
closes these rows on broker-confirmed expiry/assignment, with realised PnL
sourced from `/v2/account/activities` cash (NOT the equity local-PnL formula —
options rows are deferred in `_sweep_local_pnl_for_unpriced`).

**PnL-resolution contract (2026-06-16, live-trade management contract — full
record in the ARCHITECTURE-CANONICAL 2026-06-16 change-log row +
`docs/audits/live-trade-management-contract-2026-06-16.md`):** every account
resolves PnL the same way — **prefer broker truth, else local compute** —
keyed on `contract_value_usd` (`config/instruments.yaml`). Whether an
integration provides broker truth is a *declared capability*
(`clients.BROKER_PNL_READER_EXCHANGES`, today `{bybit}`), default-local, no
gate. So `unrealizedPnl` on `/positions` is broker-sourced when available, else
a server-side mark-to-market fallback (`unrealizedPnlSource="markprice_local"`,
multiplier-aware); and non-Bybit (IBKR/Alpaca/OANDA) *realised* PnL on
`/trades/closed` is filled by `order_monitor._sweep_local_pnl_for_unpriced`
(`notes.pnl_source="local_compute"`) rather than left NULL.

## CORS
CORS is configured in `src/web/api/main.py`. Allowed origins:
- `http://localhost:5173` (Vite dev) — legacy; no longer used by the live dashboard, harmless to leave in the list.
- `http://localhost:3000` — legacy.
- Value of `DASHBOARD_ORIGIN` env var (legacy Vercel URL — see the note below).

⚠️ **Note (rewritten 2026-09-01 — the old note is no longer true).** It used
to read: *"the Streamlit dashboard makes its upstream call server-side, so
CORS isn't load-bearing for it."* That was correct **about Streamlit**, which
is now retired from the live feed. The remaining consumer — the **Svelte SPA**
— calls the API **browser-direct** from GitHub Pages, so **CORS IS
load-bearing**: a CORS mistake breaks the only consumer there is. Do not carry
the old exemption forward.

## Environment Variables

> This table is a **curated subset** of operator-relevant toggles, not the
> full set of env vars the runtime reads. Other load-bearing runtime flags
> live in code with sensible defaults and are documented at their call
> sites — notably `MULTI_STRATEGY_INTENT_LAYER` (`intent_multiplexer.py`,
> **default on** — the core intent-aggregation switch), `RECONCILER_GRACE_SECONDS`,
> `ORPHAN_POSITION_POLICY`, `STUCK_STRATEGY_THRESHOLD_MINUTES` /
> `STUCK_STRATEGY_TIMEFRAME_MULT`, `STRATEGY_REFUSAL_COOLDOWN_SECONDS`,
> `HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_SECONDS`,
> `TICK_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, `HALT_FLAG_PATH`, and `MONITOR_BLINDNESS_ALERT_TICKS`
> (`order_monitor.py`, default `3` — consecutive ticks a position's `monitor()`
> may fail to run before the exit-coverage monitor-blindness alert fires; a
> tuning knob, not an enable gate — the alerting is always on).

| Variable | Purpose |
|----------|---------|
| `DASHBOARD_ORIGIN` | Legacy Vercel app URL — added to CORS allow-list. Was a no-op for the retired Streamlit dashboard; CORS is now load-bearing for the browser-direct Svelte SPA (which is allowed by its own GitHub Pages origin, not by this var). |
| `DASHBOARD_API_TOKEN` | Optional bearer token for auth routes |
| `SIGNAL_DUAL_WRITE_DISABLED` | When truthy, `signal_audit_logger._dual_write_to_db` skips hydrating `trade_journal.db::signals` (JSONL stays the source of truth). Default off → dual-write on. Toggle on the live VM via the `enable-signal-dual-write` / `disable-signal-dual-write` operator actions. |
| `TRADE_JOURNAL_DB` | Canonical trade-journal SQLite path (live VM: `/data/bot-data/trade_journal.db`). Resolved by the single Python resolver `src.utils.paths.trade_journal_db_path()` (env → `$DATA_DIR/trade_journal.db` → repo-root; never a CWD-relative basename). The `canonical-db-resolver` CI guard forbids re-introducing the old inline `os.environ.get("TRADE_JOURNAL_DB") or "trade_journal.db"` fallback that seeded the stray duplicate journals. |
| `TRAINER_STORE_DB` | Path to the trainer-store sidecar SQLite (default `$DATA_DIR/trainer_store.db`). Holds trainer/ML lifecycle data ingested from `runtime_logs/trainer_mirror/`; federated into the Data Explorer alongside `trade_journal.db`. Resolved by `src.utils.paths.trainer_store_db_path()`. Read-mostly — ingest writers never touch the money DB. |
| `DIAG_READ_TOKEN` | Bearer for `/api/diag/*` (read-only). Unset → endpoints return 503 |
| `M5_CONSUMER_ENABLED` / `M5_BACKTEST_TIMEOUT_S` | **REMOVED 2026-08-20 along with the M5 `/test` consumer.** A leftover value in `.env` is ignored — there is no reader. The consumer ran one hardcoded ICT engine regardless of the strategy named and wrote fabricated `0.0` metrics into `backtest_results`; it was default-off and nothing consumed its output. The `enable-m5-consumer`/`disable-m5-consumer` system-actions are gone too. Use `/api/bot/backtests/sweeps` (the trainer sweeps) for real backtest evidence. |
| `BACKTEST_DATA_PATH` | Override the candle CSV a backtest harness reads. ⚠️ **This row USED to say "the M5 backtest runner", which was wrong even before M5 was removed** — and acting on it would have broken the research fleet. Measured 2026-08-20: **23 files read this var, and 19 have nothing to do with M5** — `scripts/backtest_{chop_scalp,fade,funding_carry,fvg_range,ict_scalp,orb,pullback,squeeze,system,trend}.py`, `scripts/ml/strategy_tune_sweep.py`, `scripts/ops/{fetch_backtest_candles.py,run_serious_baseline.sh,vwap_backtest_sweep_action.sh}`, `scripts/prop/{evaluate_prop,montecarlo_prop}.py`, `scripts/research/{build_backtest_panel,build_intrabar_exit_panel,research_momentum}.py`, `src/backtest/run_backtest{,_vwap}.py`. It is a **fleet-wide** knob and survives the M5 removal untouched. |
| `VALIDATION_LOG_PATH` | **REMOVED 2026-08-20** with the M5 `/test` consumer. It was genuinely M5-only (unlike `BACKTEST_DATA_PATH` above — verified, not assumed): its sole writer was `src/utils/validation_logger.py`, called only from the consumer, and `runtime_logs/validation.jsonl` had **no read surface anywhere** (absent from `diag.py`'s `_LOG_FILES` allowlist). A write-only log with no reader. A leftover value is ignored. |
| `FLIP_POLICY` | Conflict-resolution behaviour in the intent layer when the desired net side opposes the held position (`src/runtime/intents.py`). **Default `hold` since 2026-05-31** (PR #2451, operator-approved after the 24-cell walk-forward verified PASS — `docs/audits/walkforward-flip-policy-2026-05-30.md`): keep the position; the position-owner's monitor()/SL/TP exits — removes flip-churn. Alternatives: `reverse` (legacy close-and-reopen — the rollback path; set `FLIP_POLICY=reverse` on the VM to revert without a redeploy), `flat` (close, no re-open). Mirrors `scripts/backtest_system.py --flip-policy`. |
| `FLIP_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD` | **Confidence-gap override for the hold policy** (Tier-3; `src/runtime/intents.py::resolve_flip_confidence_threshold`). Default `0.0` — **disabled, hold-policy behaviour is unchanged**. When set to a positive float (e.g. `0.15`), the `hold` policy may be overridden for a signal whose confidence exceeds the existing position's entry confidence by at least this gap (`new_conf − existing_conf ≥ threshold`). The flip is allowed only when BOTH this gap AND the age gate (`FLIP_MIN_POSITION_AGE_HOURS`) are satisfied; either alone is insufficient. Existing confidence is read from `trade_journal.db` via `get_existing_position_info` (fail-permissive: a read failure keeps the hold, never flips). Logged at INFO with `hold_confidence_override` reason prefix in the delta. **Requires operator approval (Tier-3 order-routing change) before deploying.** **LIVE VALUE: `0.0` — the override is DISARMED** (set 2026-08-11 20:14Z via `set-env`, issue #8785; **verified authoritative** in #8787 — `process: '0.0'`, `declared: '0.0'`, read from `/proc/<MainPID>/environ`, not just the `.env`). Live routing is therefore plain `FLIP_POLICY=hold`: an opposing signal never reverses a held position, whatever its confidence. `0.0` is an **early hard disarm** — `intents.py::_evaluate_confidence_override` returns at the `threshold <= 0` check *before* reading any confidence — so `FLIP_MIN_POSITION_AGE_HOURS` is inert while this is 0 (that key is deliberately left at `4.0` rather than also cleared; one key is the whole gate). **Why it was disarmed:** the walk-forward this row's predecessor recorded as never-run was finally run (2026-08-11, `docs/research/flip-override-walkforward-2026-08-11.md`, PR #8783) and the live `0.15 / 4.0` pair **lost** — net-negative vs plain `hold` across both folds. Tier-3 approval to disarm was granted by the operator in-conversation 2026-08-11. **History, so the sequence is not re-litigated:** the override ran live on real money from ~2026-08-10 with no walk-forward behind it, surfaced by `get-env` (`BL-20260810-FLIP-CONFIDENCE-OVERRIDE-LIVE-WITHOUT-RECORD`, that read surface's first find), operator-confirmed deliberate 2026-08-11, then measured and disarmed the same day (`BL-20260811-FLIP-OVERRIDE-NEVER-WALKFORWARDED`). Re-arming needs a fresh walk-forward that CLEARS plain `hold` — beating the blind-override arm is not sufficient, since that arm loses. |
| `FLIP_MIN_POSITION_AGE_HOURS` | **Minimum position age for the confidence-gap flip override** (Tier-3; `src/runtime/intents.py::resolve_flip_min_position_age_hours`). Default `0.0` — no age requirement (the gap alone is sufficient when `FLIP_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD > 0`). When set (e.g. `4.0`), the confidence override is suppressed for positions younger than this many hours, protecting fresh trades from being reversed by the next signal. Works in tandem with `FLIP_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD`: both must pass. Age is derived from `trades.created_at` (epoch-ms or ISO string); if unparseable, treated as 0h (fail-permissive — never strands a genuine old position). **LIVE VALUE: `4.0`** (measured 2026-08-10, issue #8755) — but **INERT since 2026-08-11**, because `FLIP_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD` was disarmed to `0.0` and this gate is only consulted after that threshold passes (`intents.py::_evaluate_confidence_override` returns at `threshold <= 0` before reaching the age check). It is left at `4.0` deliberately: disarming the gate takes ONE key, and clearing both would make the re-arm path ambiguous about which value was the tested one. **Do not read `4.0` as evidence the override is running** — see the `FLIP_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD` row above for the live state. |
| `REGIME_BAR_SCORING_DISABLED` | Kill-switch for the **per-bar regime scoring** path (S-MLOPT-S13 / M14 Phase 3.1, `src/runtime/regime_bar_scoring.py`). Default off → on: each tick `run_pipeline` scores every `shadow`-stage regime head on its own `(symbol,timeframe)` bar cadence (independent of any actionable signal), writing to `runtime_logs/shadow_predictions.jsonl` so the strong regime heads (1h/MES) accrue an order-influencing track record (`MB-20260529-001`). **Observe-only** — only `ShadowPredictor.predict`, never the order path; deduped to one record per closed bar. **Per-tick cost** is bounded by predictor grouping (one fetch per `(symbol, timeframe)` group, not per head) + a wall-clock fetch gate (`_BAR_SECONDS` − 30s buffer, so a 1h head is fetched ~1×/hour, a 5m head ~1×/5min) — the `MB-20260609-001` fix after the 2026-06-09 CPU wedge. Set truthy on the VM to disable without a redeploy. |
| `REGIME_BAR_SCORING_BUDGET_S` | Per-tick **wall-clock budget** (seconds, default `6.0`; `0` = unlimited) for one `emit_regime_bar_predictions` call (`src/runtime/regime_bar_scoring.py`). The fetch-gate + dedup caches are per-process and **empty on a fresh restart**, so without a budget the FIRST tick after a restart fetches every `(symbol,timeframe)` group (incl. blocking IBKR fetches for MES) AND scores every shadow head in one synchronous mega-tick — pegging the 2-core live VM and freezing the heartbeat (the `BL-20260609-001` 2026-06-10 **cold-start** wedge, distinct from the steady-state `MB-20260609-001` fix). The budget caps how long one call may run; once exceeded, remaining **whole** groups are deferred to the next tick (their fetch gate stays un-armed + heads stay unseen, so each is picked up intact later), spreading the cold-start burst across ticks instead of stalling the loop. |
| `REGIME_ROUTER_DISABLED` | **Kill-switch for the regime hard gate** (`src/runtime/intents.py::_regime_router_active`). The router is **BASELINE-ON** since the Design-A vol-gate go-live (2026-06-28): a *required* live order-routing capability must not sit behind a default-off `*_ENABLED` flag (Prime Directive) — if such a var were dropped on a redeploy/VM-migration the gate would silently stop enforcing and the money-losing `trend_vol` OFF-cells would trade again (the netting-guard / Ampere failure class). By default `Coordinator.aggregate_intents` drops every OFF-cell candidate intent (per `config/regime_policy.yaml`) BEFORE the reinforcement / conflict-resolution logic runs and emits a `regime_hard_gate` audit row with `enforced:true`; the `regime_shadow_gate` row is **not** emitted on the same tick, so the audit log cleanly partitions "would have gated" from "did gate" by event name. Set `REGIME_ROUTER_DISABLED` truthy → off (shadow-log only, `enforced:false`) — the sanctioned rollback (one env flip + restart, no redeploy). **Legacy:** a leftover *explicit* falsy `REGIME_ROUTER_ENABLED` (`0`/`false`) still disables (honoured so a VM mid-migration with the old var set isn't surprised); the live VM's `REGIME_ROUTER_ENABLED=true` is now redundant (still on, harmless). The backtest harness sets `REGIME_ROUTER_DISABLED=1` on any non-`--regime-router on` run so the A/B baseline arm stays shadow-only. Fail-permissive on any policy load / verdict exception (keeps the intent). |
| `ACCOUNT_CONTEXT_SNAPSHOTS_DISABLED` | Kill-switch for the **per-signal account-context snapshot writer** (S-MLOPT-S12 Part B / M14 Phase 2.4, `src/units/accounts/context_snapshot.py` + `src/core/coordinator.py::_capture_account_context_snapshots`). Default off → on: `Coordinator.multi_account_execute` writes one row per `(order_package_id, eligible_account)` into `trade_journal.db::account_context_snapshots` BEFORE the per-account RiskManager runs — capturing equity, daily PnL, daily equity-high, drawdown%, and open-trade count as they stood pre-decision. **Observe-only** — the snapshot feeds the optional `include_snapshots=True` LEFT JOIN in the `account_context` family (closes `MB-20260604-003`); the trader's order flow never reads back from this table. Best-effort writer (swallows all exceptions); set truthy on the VM to disable without a redeploy. |
| `CROSS_ASSET_LIVE_DISABLED` | Kill-switch for the **live cross-asset peer-feature computation** (S-CROSS-ASSET-PROBE D2a, `src/runtime/cross_asset_live.py`). Default off → on: the per-bar regime scorer (`regime_bar_scoring.py`) computes the `xa_*` peer-asset feature block (peers per `config/cross_asset.yaml`, e.g. `ETHUSDT: [BTCUSDT, SOLUSDT]`) at score time for any cross-asset regime head (one whose feature list has `xa_*` cols), reusing the offline pure fns so live==train. **Observe-only** — only conditions a shadow-stage head's features (→ `shadow_predictions.jsonl`); never the order path. Peers ride the target's existing gated fetch cadence (no new fetch rate); fail-permissive — but the two failure scopes are NOT the same and were previously described as if they were (corrected 2026-08-20). A **whole-row** failure (peer fetch/compute raises) returns `None`, the caller merges nothing, and the head scores its xa columns as **NaN** — the honest degraded state, never a fabricated zero vector. **Within a SUCCESSFUL row an absent or return-less peer slot emits `0.0`**, which is a real value the head reads, so the `xa_peer{n}_present` flag (and the book-level `xa_breadth_present` denominator) ship beside them — `ml/datasets/cross_asset_features.py`, 13→16 columns, 2026-08-20 — so `0.0` can be told apart from *"we did not look"*. `xa_breadth_up` deliberately KEEPS its zero-when-empty value (trained heads read it); read it beside `xa_breadth_present` or a book where no peer had a return is indistinguishable from one where every peer fell. Set truthy on the VM to disable without a redeploy (the peer config then resolves empty → the feature merge is a no-op). The eventual `c_reg` conviction contribution (D2b) is operator/backtest-gated. |
| `REGIME_ML_VERDICT_MODE` / `ML_VOL_VERDICT_THRESHOLD` | **Design-A regime-router ML vol-verdict** (`off` (default) / `shadow` / `use`; `src/runtime/regime/ml_vol_verdict.py` + `intents.py`, PR #4748, 2026-06-27). Drives the `vol_regime` axis from the **advisory** regime head's `P(volatile)` (thresholded at `ML_VOL_VERDICT_THRESHOLD`, default `0.5`) instead of the frozen-edge `vol_detector`. `off`→unchanged; `shadow`→emit a `regime_ml_vol_shadow` agreement audit row (frozen-vs-ML), decision unchanged; `use`→**substitute the advisory head's ML vol label into the gate DECISION** via `intents._decision_vol_regime` (actually wired 2026-06-28; before that `use` was a documented placeholder that still used the frozen label). **For `use` to change a real-money outcome ALL three must hold:** (a) an OFF cell exists for the `(trend, vol)` pair (`trend_vol` cells ARE authored — merged #4868); (b) the gated strategy's **SYMBOL** has an advisory regime head — resolution is **per-SYMBOL** (`ml_vol_regime_for_symbol`), NOT per-`(symbol, timeframe)`: BTC's advisory 15m head is **`btc-regime-15m-lgbm-fc-pcv-v2`** (swapped from `btc-regime-15m-lgbm-fc-pcv-v1`, demoted advisory→shadow, on 2026-08-04 — operator-approved on the gate packet in trainer-diag #8433/#8435/#8436 and registry-verified #8448; the v1 head had itself replaced `btc-regime-15m-lgbm-v2` in the M25 promotion 2026-07-20, `docs/sprint-logs/S-M25-PROMOTION-EXECUTION-20260720.md`), so **every** BTC cell — incl. `trend_donchian` (1h) + `squeeze_breakout_4h` (4h) — resolves the ML label (confirmed live), **not** frozen; a symbol with no advisory head resolves `unknown`→frozen (permissive); and (c) the hard gate is active (baseline-on; kill-switch `REGIME_ROUTER_DISABLED`). Fail-permissive (ML unknown / no head / exception → keep the frozen label, never strands a signal). The 4-arm A/B (`docs/research/A-vol-gating-AB-evidence-2026-06-27.md`) showed the ML label beats the frozen label decisively. **Tier-3** (order-routing-affecting); `use` + the threshold are operator-gated, walk-forward-gated. **BTC real-money enforce is LIVE** (2026-06-28, head swapped 2026-07-20). **SOL advisory head RESTORED** — `sol-regime-15m-lgbm-fc-pcv-v2` was **promoted shadow→advisory 2026-08-02** (operator-approved; gate packet #8294 `ready:true` — oos_edge +0.239, drift clean KS 0.1353/PSI 0.0391, live_parity 50/0, 11.6d soak), replacing the demoted `-v1` (advisory→shadow 2026-07-26 on `drift_clean` KS=0.236). **No SOL `trend_vol` cell is authored, and the evidence says none SHOULD be** (not a gap — the correct state): the 2026-07-06 walk-forward vol-split (`docs/research/A-vol-gating-ETH-SOL-OFFcell-evidence-2026-07-06.md`, a clean backtest — NOT the fabricated-journal PnL class) found SOL a **clean negative** — its ungated book is the healthiest of the three symbols (ret/DD 1.72, all folds profitable), the gate LOSES net (full-history −$198) and only buys drawdown, and the negative cells FAIL the walk-forward (fixed-cell 2/4, cell-selection 1/3; small-n, sign-flip OOS). So the SOL advisory head is an **observer with no cell** (zero live-order impact). A better vol *label* cannot make a healthy all-folds-profitable book need gating; re-visit SOL cell authoring only if a retrained head + a fresh walk-forward proves a money-losing cell that GENERALIZES (authoring one on the current evidence would be the cosmetic-cell anti-pattern, `BL-20260730-DONCHIAN-COSMETIC-SHORT-CELLS`). ETH's 15m head is still shadow, awaiting its own promotion (`MB-20260628-VOLGATE-GOLIVE`). The `-v2` fresh-data swap status (`MB-20260720-FCPCV-RETRAIN-NOOP` / `MB-20260721-FCPCV-V2-SOAK`): **BOTH `-v2` siblings swapped in — the fresh-data refresh is COMPLETE**: SOL 2026-08-02 (gate packet #8294), BTC 2026-08-04 (gate packet #8433/#8435 — drift_clean finally cleared at KS 0.1269 after hovering 0.20–0.25 across 08-01/08-02; PSI 0.2093 is nearer its 0.25 bar than SOL's, worth watching on the next reviews). Both v1 heads sit demoted at shadow. |
| `CONVICTION_SIZING_MODE` / `CONVICTION_SIZING_DIRECTION` / `CONVICTION_SIZING_ACCOUNTS` | **Design-B conviction-driven sizing apply path** (`off` (default) / `annotate` / `apply`; `src/runtime/conviction_sizing.py::apply_conviction_sizing` + `coordinator.py`, PR #4748). A NEW apply path (distinct from the flagless annotator soak), gating a real reductive/symmetric size influence — same role `NEWS_INFLUENCE_MODE` plays (passes `env-gate-guard` as a `*_MODE`, not the previously-rejected `*_ENABLED`). `off`→byte-for-byte unchanged. `DIRECTION ∈ {reductive (default), symmetric}`; `ACCOUNTS` allowlist (**empty = ALL accounts, including real money** — an empty allowlist is not a safe default, it is the widest one). **LIVE VALUE (per `ROADMAP.md` M16, operator-approved 2026-08-05): `MODE=apply` / `DIRECTION=reductive` / `ACCOUNTS=bybit_1` — the reductive apply path is LIVE, and DEMO-ONLY.** *(Corrected 2026-08-10: this row previously read "→ stays at `off`/annotate", which was true only of the SYMMETRIC variant and had been overtaken by the 2026-08-05 reductive flip. `ROADMAP.md` outranks this file — read the live state there, not here.)* The **symmetric** c_strat-only variant did fail its A/B (4.5× worse maxDD) and stays off. The C1 deployable-evidence A/B (365d, per-symbol, net-of-cost, 2026-08-05) came back **PARTIAL** — reductive cuts maxDD on BTC but worsens it on SOL, over a net-negative population — so widening beyond `bybit_1` needs a clean cross-symbol win at solid n on a floor-clearing population. Tier-3; every widening is operator-gated + backtest-gated. |
| `NETTING_ATTRIBUTION_MODE` / `NETTING_ATTRIBUTION_ACCOUNTS` | **Netting partial-close ATTRIBUTION** (`annotate` (default) / `apply`; `order_monitor._reconcile_netting_partial_closes`, BL-20260801-NETTING-PARTIAL-CLOSE-ROWS-NEVER-REDUCED; design `docs/netting-partial-close-attribution-DESIGN.md`). Under one-way netting a symbol is ONE exchange position holding N journal rows; a position-level exit shrinks that single position but close detection is **per-ORDER** (`_reconcile_open_trades` reconciles each row against *its own* Bybit order), so siblings keep their full `position_size` forever — measured 2026-08-06 at **451×** on `bybit_1` SOLUSDT (journal 2075.2 vs exchange 4.6), while every non-netting venue is exactly clean. This reconciler attributes the reduction: **leg-id first** (a tracked SL leg no longer resting names a specific trade — *evidence, not proof*, since absence cannot distinguish fired from cancelled), **FIFO for the residual**, and a row whose leg is **still resting is picked LAST** (a live leg is evidence the exchange still backs that qty). **Provenance ladder (operator directive 2026-08-06 — prefer a stated estimate over declaring nothing, but an estimate REQUIRES an anchor):** `anchored` → price from the bar at the divergence's **first observation** and stamp `ANCHOR_SOURCE` (**ESTIMATED**) · `no_anchor` → close carrying `UNMEASURED_MARKER`, never a substituted price (an anchorless "estimate" is FABRICATED — the class behind the phantom −$6,358 leak) · `deferred` → retry, write nothing. The anchor is the **first observation, not "now"** — anchoring a row that may have closed days ago to the current bar is exactly the sweep-time-mark fabrication `_sweep_local_pnl_for_unpriced` was fixed to stop; for the pre-existing backlog it is only an UPPER BOUND and the row records that (`netting_anchor_basis`). A `*_MODE` var, not a default-off `*_ENABLED` gate (the `NEWS_INFLUENCE_MODE` / `CONVICTION_SIZING_MODE` shape): at `annotate` the reconciler does **all** the work and writes the rows it WOULD reduce to `runtime_logs/netting_attribution_soak.jsonl` without touching the money DB. `ACCOUNTS` is an optional CSV allowlist that scopes **the WRITE, never the measurement** (empty = all Bybit may be written). Every Bybit account is observed and annotated to the soak log regardless; only an allowlisted one can have `apply` touch the money DB (`order_monitor._netting_may_write`, the single place the allowlist is consulted). **Corrected 2026-08-09** — it previously intersected the account set at the top of the pass (`bybit_ids &= allow`), so staging on `bybit_1` (operator decision 4) also made real-money **`bybit_2` invisible**: no divergence check, no soak row, nothing to review before widening the allowlist to it — while `bybit_2` had been measured **non-clean** on 2026-08-06. A staging control that disables measurement of the thing you are staging toward is self-defeating, and it is the same conflation of "no policy here" with "no data here" that the gross-exposure ceiling had. The soak row now carries the **effective** `mode` (what happened to that row) beside `global_mode` (what was asked for) and `apply_scope` (`no_allowlist` / `allowlisted` / `not_allowlisted` — why they differ), so a held-back row can never read as an applied one; the run summary counts `apply_suppressed_by_allowlist` so "staged" stays distinguishable from "nothing to do". Fail-safe: an unreadable exchange read is skipped (never attribute on an unconfirmed read), pairs-sleeve rows are excluded (that executor owns its own state), and the 2-observation confirm reuses `RECONCILER_CLOSE_CONFIRM_SECONDS`. **Tier-2 to flip to `apply`.** |
| `POSITION_NETTING_GUARD_ENABLED` | **Removed 2026-06-17 — the netting guard is now BASELINE (unconditional).** It was a default-OFF kill-switch over a *correctness* fix (per-trade=per-position: monocle no-pyramiding via `src/core/coordinator.py::multi_account_execute` + `positions.py::has_open_trade_for_strategy`; reconciler 2-observation close-confirm via `order_monitor.py::_reconcile_open_trades`). Per the Prime Directive a required capability must not sit behind a default-off flag — and that gate let the fix silently regress when the 2026-06-14 Ampere migration dropped the `.env` var (paper netting artifacts reappeared 2026-06-15, real-money `bybit_2` exposed). Now always-on (`positions.py::position_netting_guard_active_for` returns True unconditionally); a leftover value in `.env` is **ignored**. Same class as the removed `NAKED_POSITION_AUTOPROTECT` / `MONITOR_RECONCILE_ENABLED` gates. Still journals a `reentry_suppressed_netting_guard:<action>` rejection row when it suppresses a netted add. |
| `POSITION_NETTING_GUARD_ACCOUNTS` | **Removed 2026-06-17** alongside `POSITION_NETTING_GUARD_ENABLED` — the guard is baseline on every account (a no-op where it can't apply, e.g. brokers that attach SL/TP atomically and never net same-direction adds), so there is no scope to narrow. A leftover value in `.env` is **ignored**. |
| `RECONCILER_CLOSE_CONFIRM_SECONDS` | Tuning knob (default `60`, clamped `>= 0`) for the **2-observation close-confirm** shared by TWO reconciler close paths: (a) the **netting-guard** half (`_reconcile_open_trades`, now unconditional — the netting guard is baseline as of 2026-06-17) — min seconds a filled trade must read net-flat across ≥2 observations before it closes; and (b) the **reverse reconciler's orphan close-on-disappear** (`_reconcile_orphan_exchange_positions`, BL-20260614-ORPHANBLIP) — an `orphan_adopt` row that reads absent from the exchange snapshot must stay absent across ≥2 observations this many seconds apart before it closes, so a logged-out IB Gateway's empty-portfolio blip can't close + re-orphan it. **(b) is always on** (the monitor reconciler / self-heal runs unconditionally on every tick — the `MONITOR_RECONCILE_ENABLED` gate was removed 2026-06-15, BL-20260615-MGCNAKED — and (b) is NOT gated by the netting guard either — baseline correctness, not a feature flag); this knob only tunes the window. `0` keeps the extra-grace-tick requirement (a second confirming observation) with no added time wait. Read at call time (next-tick effect). Sibling of `RECONCILER_GRACE_SECONDS`. |
| `RECONCILER_READOPT_GUARD_SECONDS` | Window (seconds, **default `1800`** — widened from `300` on 2026-07-19, BL-20260618 residual; clamped `>= 0`; `0` disables) for the **re-adopt flap guard** in the reverse reconciler (`_reconcile_orphan_exchange_positions`, BL-20260618-RECONCILE-DUP). When an IB gateway flaps during the broker reset window (logged-out → empty portfolio → back), or the re-attached strategy's monitor closes the DB row at an `sl_cross` on a position the broker never actually closed, the still-present exchange position would be **re-adopted next pass** — looping N times into N phantom `adopted_orphan` trades (one MGC position became 18 closed trades, −$20,127). The guard refuses to re-adopt a `(account, symbol, direction)` whose `adopted_orphan` row (`setup_type='adopted_orphan'`, covers both the bare and strategy-reattached paths) closed within this window — a just-closed adopted orphan that reappears is a flap, not a new position; it's suppressed + alerted (`detect_only`) instead, the real exchange position staying operator-/SL-protected. **The 300s default let a flap SLOWER than 5 min re-adopt a single duplicate (the real-money double-count residual); 1800s covers the full IBKR reset window. Safe to widen because the guard suppresses-AND-alerts (never silently strands) and the adopt path only fires for un-matched orphan positions (a real strategy position carries a journal row and never reaches this guard).** Fail-open (a read error never blocks a genuine adoption). Read at call time (next-tick effect). Sibling of `RECONCILER_CLOSE_CONFIRM_SECONDS` (the close-side confirm). |
| `RECONCILER_SNAPSHOT_MIN_FILL_AGE_S` | **Fresh-fill grace** (seconds, default `300`, clamped `>= 0`; `0` disables) for the P3b position-snapshot reconciler (`_reconcile_orphan_exchange_positions`, BL-20260622-ALPACA-SNAPSHOT-FALSECLOSE). On an integration without a per-order status reader (alpaca/oanda), a just-placed bracket-MARKET order can take minutes to fill AND propagate to the open-positions snapshot — during that window the position reads **absent** yet is NOT flat (it's pending fill).
