IB is memoized on its **RESOLVED connection identity** (`_ib_connection_identity`: host/port/clientId/account, resolved settings → env → `accounts.yaml`), not on the settings dict, so the memo key cannot drift from what would actually be constructed; an unresolvable endpoint (no `ib_port` anywhere) still refuses to memo. **It adds NO new socket sharing** — the live-IB-client count per process is unchanged for the reason above, so BL-20260706-IBACCTUPDATES-COLLISION is untouched. **Measured on the live trader** (`/api/diag/tick_cost`; **after** = one process `23:08:50Z→04:48:04Z`, n=676 passes; **before** = n=433): off-loop `fetch.15m` **1.002 → 0.494 per pass**, mean exit pass **42.3s → 4.89s (8.6×)**, `EXIT_EVAL_MAX_INTERVAL_SECONDS` breaches **28.9% → 5/675 = 0.74%** of passes. ⚠️ **State the population when quoting the breach ratio:** these counters are **per-process** and the 28.9% pre-figure's own process was not recorded beside it, so the two rates are not guaranteed to share a denominator — the direction and order of magnitude are solid, the precise ~39× is approximate. ⚠️ **The 60s requirement is still BREACHED at the tail** (`max_interval_ms` 88.76s) — the residual is `BL-20260816-IB-QUEUE-TIMEOUT-EXCEEDS-EXIT-BUDGET`, a different cause; do not read the 8.6× as closing it. (b) the CANDLE cache is TTL-bounded per above, keyed on `(client instance, symbol, timeframe, limit)` so two venues serving the same symbol string never share, **never caches a `since=` request** (a historical-range reconstruction goes to the venue every time), skips unknown timeframes, and hands every caller a `.copy()` so a builder adding an indicator column cannot corrupt the next builder's frame. |
| `EXPOSURE_SOAK_SECONDS` | Sampling cadence (seconds, default `900` = 15 min; `<= 0` pauses) of the **gross-exposure observation soak** (`src/runtime/exposure_soak.py::emit_exposure_soak`, called once per trader tick from `src/main.py`). Writes one `runtime_logs/exposure_soak.jsonl` row per declared account per window — the account's own `RiskManager.report()["exposure"]`, never a reconstruction (a second definition of "exposure" would be free to drift from the one that governs). **A cadence knob, NOT an enable gate** — the `ACCOUNT_REACHABILITY_CHECK_SECONDS` / `PROP_MONITOR_PULSE_SECONDS` shape; a required observability capability must not sit behind a default-off `*_ENABLED` flag (Prime Directive), and an unparseable value **falls back to the default rather than pausing**, so a typo cannot silently switch observation off. Observe-only, connection-free, best-effort (never raises into the tick). Surfaced at `/api/bot/exposure/soak` + `/api/diag/log_file?name=exposure_soak`. |
| `EXIT_LOOP_DECOUPLE_DISABLED` / `EXIT_LOOP_INTERVAL_SECONDS` / `EXIT_LOOP_STALE_SECONDS` / `EXIT_EVAL_MAX_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | **The M20 exit-loop decouple** (`src/main.py::_exit_loop` + `src/runtime/exit_loop_health.py`, Tier-2 operator-approved, shipped + live-verified 2026-08-12 — `docs/sprint-logs/S-M20-EXIT-LOOP-DECOUPLE-2026-08-12.md`). Exit evaluation runs on its own **daemon loop** instead of inline on the trader tick, so a live trade is re-evaluated on the exit loop's cadence rather than once per tick. ⚠️ **DO NOT QUOTE THE GO-LIVE MARGIN — IT NO LONGER HOLDS** (corrected 2026-08-16, `BL-20260816-EXIT-EVAL-INTERVAL-AT-60S-REQUIREMENT`). This row previously read "re-evaluated every **30.2 s observed** … worst observed interval **34.1 s**, clearing the operator's 60 s ask by 43.1%". That was true at go-live and is **stale in the dangerous direction**: measured 2026-08-16T05:39Z over **n=694** passes on one process, `monitor.strategy_monitor_loop` is **28852.0 ms mean / 58940.8 ms max**. Because the interval is `max(interval, pass)` (see the period-targeting note below), the **worst observed inter-evaluation interval is 58.9 s against a 60 s requirement — 1.1 s of margin, not 43%.** ⚠️ **DO NOT REPEAT THE "MOVED ONE WAY" READING EITHER** (corrected 2026-08-16, same day, on a second process). This row briefly said "the series has moved one way throughout: 22.4 s → 34.1 s → 26.6 s → 58.9 s". **That series is not valid evidence of a trend, for two reasons.** (1) It **mixes means with maxima** as if they were one quantity: separated, the *means* are 22.4 s (n=55) → 30.2 s (go-live) → 26.6 s (n=219) → 28.9 s (n=694) → **21.6 s (n=32)**, a range of 21.6–30.2 s that goes up, down, up, down — flat, with the newest process the LOWEST of the five. The *maxima* are three points: 34.1 s, 58.9 s, 41.5 s. (2) It **compares maxima across different n**, and a maximum grows with sample size even from a fixed distribution — so `max(n=694) > max(n=38)` is the expected result of drawing more samples, not evidence of degradation. **What survives is that 58940.8 ms was really observed**: a live trade did go 58.9 s unevaluated. The supported diagnosis is a **fat tail finally sampled**, not a system creeping toward the requirement — which points at bounding the tail (`BL-20260816-IB-QUEUE-TIMEOUT-EXCEEDS-EXIT-BUDGET`) rather than hunting for what got slower. ✅ **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-25 AND THE TAIL WAS BOUND — the 58.9 s / 1.1 s-margin figures above are HISTORY, do not quote them as current.** Population, because it is the whole point: **n = 991 intervals across 10 processes over 8.3 h** (09:37Z→17:56Z), read from `/api/diag/log_file?name=exit_interval_soak` — the DURABLE per-pass soak, not a single process's in-memory max, so this is the first reading not subject to the "no process lived long enough to draw the tail" caveat two sentences above. `interval_ms` **29879 mean / 29970 median / 33476 p95 / 45034 MAX**, and `over_requirement` **0 of 991 (0.00%)** — so the worst observed inter-evaluation interval is **45.0 s against the 60 s requirement, 15.0 s of margin**. The pass itself is **2222 ms mean / 19481 ms max**, against 28852 ms mean / 58940.8 ms max on 2026-08-16 — a **13×** improvement in the mean, from #10114's IB connector memo plus `IB_PROBE_CACHE_S`. The interval is now bounded by the **30 s cadence**, not by the pass, which is the regime the design intends. ⚠️ **TWO THINGS THIS DOES NOT SAY.** (1) It is a 1000-row TAIL of a 6.29 MB file, so it is the last 8.3 h and not the lifetime; the max over the whole file may be higher. (2) A 45.0 s interval is NOT explained by the 19.5 s max pass — `max(cadence, pass)` would cap at 30 s — so ~15 s came from a stall BETWEEN the sleep and the next completion, exactly the gap the measured completion-to-completion interval exists to catch and the derivation would miss. Small against the requirement today; it is the term to watch if the margin shrinks again. **The MAX is the quantity the requirement is written against, not the mean** — a mean-only reading is what let this drift unremarked, and it is why `BL-20260814-EXIT-PASS-SLOWER-THAN-M20` was closed while the max kept growing. **The requirement is now GRADED, and this is the second, separate threshold** (shipped 2026-08-16 — before that, nothing alarmed on it: `exit_loop_health` graded only `fresh`/`stale` on `EXIT_LOOP_STALE_SECONDS`, so a 59 s pass read healthy and the requirement was observable only by reading `max_ms` out of `/api/diag/tick_cost` by hand). `EXIT_EVAL_MAX_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default `60`) is the **requirement**; `EXIT_LOOP_STALE_SECONDS` (180 s) is **liveness**. They are deliberately different questions and must never be collapsed into one number — at 180 s a 59 s interval and a 179 s interval are both `fresh`. `exit_loop_health` now MEASURES the completion-to-completion interval (**not** the `max(interval, pass)` derivation — a stall between the sleep and the next completion is invisible to the derivation) and publishes `max_interval_ms` + a four-state **`requirement_state`** ∈ `within` / `breached` / `not_measured` (fewer than 2 passes, so no interval EXISTS — never `within`) / `unknown`, registered with `collapsed-state-guard`. **`state: fresh` and `requirement_state: breached` can coexist**, which is exactly the condition that was previously unrepresentable. A breach alerts **once per PROCESS** — `max_interval_ms` resets on restart, so a global latch would go silent after the first breach ever. ⚠️ **Read `requirement_state` beside `intervals_measured`, never alone** (the `max_multiple`/`measured_n` discipline): the grade is per-process and the trader restarts on every merge to `main` — three processes in ~8.5 h on 2026-08-16 — while the tail needs a large `n` to be drawn at all (the 58.9 s observation came from an n=694 overnight process; the two daytime processes reached n=38 and n=23, maxing at 41.5 s and 47.4 s). On a busy day `within` can mean *"no process lived long enough to draw the tail"*, **not** *"the requirement was met today"*. The pass is **fetch-bound** (off-loop `fetchby.strategy_monitor_loop` n=10034, the largest fetch consumer anywhere; off-loop `fetch.1d` mean 10720.9 ms), so this shares a root with the tick regression and a TTL/fetch change aimed at the tick reaches **exit decisions** too, in both directions. **Only the EXIT half moved** — the 13 reconcilers stay on the tick, because only the exit half is what the 60 s ask is about. `EXIT_LOOP_DECOUPLE_DISABLED` truthy is the **sanctioned rollback: one env flip + restart, no redeploy** — no thread, the tick calls `run_monitor_tick`, byte-for-byte the old behaviour. `EXIT_LOOP_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default `30`) is **period-targeting, not sleep-after** (`slack = interval − elapsed`), so the inter-evaluation interval is `max(interval, pass)` and a pass that overruns starts the next immediately rather than queueing — the cadence is a floor on frequency, never a schedule to catch up on (piling passes onto one shared IB socket is how the June 2026 wedges started). `EXIT_LOOP_STALE_SECONDS` (default `180`) is when `exit_loop_health` grades the loop `stale`. **BOTH DEFAULTS ARE CHOSEN, NOT MEASURED** — bounded by the 22.4 s mean pass with one 55-pass distribution behind them; do not read them as tuned values. The loop's liveness is **only** observable via `exit_loop_health` (`/api/diag/log_file?name=exit_loop_health`), because the decouple took exit evaluation OUT of the liveness watchdog's coverage — that coverage was never a probe, it was the fact that exit evaluation ran INLINE on the tick whose heartbeat the watchdog measures. Its four **liveness** states are never collapsed: `unknown` / `never_ran` (**emphatically NOT healthy** — also what a set `EXIT_LOOP_DECOUPLE_DISABLED` produces) / `fresh` / `stale`; read them beside the four **requirement** states described above, which answer a different question. |
| `TRADE_OPEN_BANNER_WINDOW_MIN` | Window (minutes, default `30`) for the best-effort **trade_open** info banner on `GET /api/bot/notifications` — a compact "N trades opened in the last Nm" line so a fresh entry surfaces on the app Overview banner. Read-only DB query, best-effort (any failure omits the banner). |
| `WORK_DECISION_PROMPT_SECONDS` / `WORK_DECISION_API_BASE` / `WORK_DECISION_PROMPT_RETAIN_DAYS` | Knobs for the **two-way Telegram decision channel** (`src/runtime/telegram_decisions.py`, operator-requested 2026-09-02: *"if there's decisions that are pop-ups they can be given to me as pop-ups in the telegram chat ... instead of having to see the ping and then open the session"*). A sweep on `ict-telegram-bot.service`'s job queue reads `GET /api/bot/work/decisions` and sends ONE inline-keyboard prompt per un-prompted, `not_submitted` request; a tap POSTs to `/api/bot/work/decision` over loopback with the `DASHBOARD_API_TOKEN` bearer. ⚠️ **IT DECIDES NOTHING AND THE CONFIRMATION SAYS SO** — the route returns `answerState: in_transit`, so the reply after a tap reads *"Submitted — NOT yet decided"* and names the object YAML where truth lands. A confirmation that said *answered* would be the forward failure the transit contract exists to refuse. ⚠️ **THE ROUTE STAYS THE ONE OWNER OF EVERY REFUSAL** — unknown option (400), empty submission (400), already-answered (**409**, rendered to the operator as *already answered*, not as an error), fail-closed write gate (503). The bot re-implements none of them; it translates the answer for a human. ⚠️ **`callback_data` is 26 bytes FIXED — `wdec:<12 hex>:<8 hex>`** against Telegram's 64-byte cap, digesting `(object_id, request_id)` and the option **KEY**. The option token is a key digest and **never a positional index**: an index is stable only while nobody edits the object file, so a reordering between the send and the tap would silently record a DIFFERENT answer than the one whose label the operator read; a key digest fails loudly (`option_gone`). A digest collision is **refused**, never resolved by first match. ⚠️ **MULTIPLE CHOICE ONLY — free text is a second step and is deliberately not half-built.** Inline buttons cannot carry arbitrary text; a request declaring no options is still SENT (so a question the operator is blocking on is not invisible) but says plainly that it needs an answer on the SPA or in the repo, and the two populations are counted separately in the sweep stats. ⚠️ **DESTINATION IS THE *POLLED* BOT, NOT `claude_route()`** — measured 2026-09-02, `ict-telegram-bot.service` polls `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` and `ict-claude-bridge.service` polls the **prop** token, while `TELEGRAM_CLAUDE_BOT_SECRET` (the dedicated Claude bot) is polled by **nothing**; a prompt sent there would render buttons that silently go nowhere. Delivery and ANSWERABILITY are different properties and `telegram_routes` only reasons about the first, which is why `telegram_decisions.answerable_route()` exists beside it rather than reusing it. `..._SECONDS` (default `300`) is the cadence and `<= 0` PAUSES prompting; an unparseable value falls back to the **default, never to zero** — a typo must not silently switch the decision channel off. `..._API_BASE` (default `http://127.0.0.1:8001`) is where the sweep reads and writes; `..._RETAIN_DAYS` (default `30`) bounds the prompted-marker file. Cadence/scope knobs, **not** a default-off `*_ENABLED` gate (Prime Directive). Marker state at `/api/diag/log_file?name=work_decision_prompted`. ⚠️ **THE SWEEP IS NO LONGER THE ONLY WAY IN — `/decisions` PULLS THE SAME INBOX ON DEMAND** (2026-09-02, operator-requested: *"if there's any decisions that are still waiting for me that aren't answered so they can all pop up at once. In case I don't get them as they come in"*). It reuses the SAME `render_decision_prompt` / `build_decision_keyboard` builders, so there is still exactly one author of `callback_data`. ⚠️ **IT NEITHER READS NOR WRITES THE PROMPTED MARKER, DELIBERATELY** — not reading is the point of the ask (it must re-send what the sweep already asked), and not writing keeps the sweep's once-only behaviour intact: writing a marker here would SUPPRESS the sweep for a question the operator pulled, so a failed on-demand send would leave it asked by nobody. The accepted consequence is stated rather than hidden — a pulled question may ALSO arrive once from the sweep — because a duplicate PROMPT is recoverable and a suppressed one is not, and the route's 409 makes a duplicate DECISION impossible. **Tier-2** (it touches the Telegram bridge and needs an `ict-telegram-bot.service` restart); no order path, no strategy config, no risk caps. |
| `PROTECTION_REASSERT_MODE` / `PROTECTION_REASSERT_ACCOUNTS` / `PROTECTION_REASSERT_COOLDOWN_S` / `PROTECTION_REASSERT_MAX_ATTEMPTS` | **Re-assert a diverged protective leg at its journal-declared level** (`off` / `annotate` (default) / `apply`; `src/runtime/protection_reassert.py` + `order_monitor._reassert_from_divergence`, operator-directed 2026-08-23: *"we need to be able to adjust trades on IB without disconnecting the integration ... we can't leave it to chance because of one trade not being worth the effort"*). ⚠️ **THE MECHANISM ALWAYS EXISTED; ONLY THE TRIGGER WAS MISSING** — `IBClient.modify_protective` runs on the TRADER'S OWN client, so it needs no ops clientId and evicts nothing. What blocked it was structural: `interpret_verdict` is passed `current_sl=open_pkg.get("sl")` — **the JOURNAL** — and the venue's resting price is never read on that path, so the strategy recomputes its level, the journal already says that level, the filter drops it as `no_meaningful_change`, and nothing is sent. Once journal and venue diverge the divergence is **PERMANENT BY CONSTRUCTION** (`BL-20260823-MODIFY-IDEMPOTENCE-COMPARES-INTENT-TO-JOURNAL-NEVER-TO-VENUE`). Motivating instance, **now RESOLVED — do not re-quote it as live** (verified 2026-08-29): `ib_paper` MES 4350 declared 7533.69642857 while its only resting stop sat at 7516.50 — 69 ticks, **$1,289.73** on 15 contracts — held from 2026-08-20 with a healthy monitor on a connected session. Measured now via `/api/diag/ib_open_orders`, that position rests STP **7533.75** + LMT 8390.50 in ONE OCA group, qty 15 against a 15-lot position, matching the journal to the 0.25 tick on BOTH legs. ⚠️ **This gate cannot be credited for that, and reading it as a success would be exactly the unprovenanced conclusion this file warns about**: `PROTECTION_REASSERT_MODE` ships at `annotate` and `PROTECTION_REASSERT_ACCOUNTS` is empty, which means NONE here — so the decision ran and cancelled/re-placed nothing. What closed the gap is NOT established; the candidates are an ordinary re-arm on a subsequent open or a naked-sweep repair, and neither was observed. So the row's STRUCTURAL claim is untouched and still the point — an idempotence filter comparing intent to the JOURNAL rather than the VENUE makes a divergence permanent by construction — while its one live exhibit is gone. **The gate is therefore still UNEXERCISED**: no soak row has yet recorded a `reassert` verdict on a real position, so it is deployed, not proven. **It rides the cadence-gated naked sweep** (`IB_BROKER_NAKED_CHECK_SECONDS`, 300s), which ALREADY fetches the resting prices, so it adds **no broker read** — the obvious alternative (compare against the venue inside the per-pass modify path) would put a broker call on every open position every pass, which is the shape of both June 2026 wedges. **The DECISION is a pure function** so the policy is arguable in tests rather than against a live position — which is exactly what went wrong on 2026-08-20 (`BL-20260820-OVERCOVER-REMEDIATION-CANCELLED-THE-JOURNAL-MATCHING-LEG`). Seven states, never collapsed: `reassert` · `agrees` · `not_graded` (**we did not look** — an unresolvable tick or unreadable resting price is NOT agreement) · `suppressed_cooldown` · `suppressed_attempts` · `needs_both_legs` · `position_absent`. ⚠️ **THE LEVEL IS ALWAYS THE JOURNAL'S** (`trades.stop_loss` / `take_profit_1`), never a caller's or an operator's — recording the venue's stray level as the new declaration would launder it into the journal and make the divergence unfindable. ⚠️ **BOTH LEGS OR NEITHER**: `modify_protective` re-arms the WHOLE bracket, so a re-assert carrying only the stop would drop a resting target; a missing declared level is a REFUSAL (`needs_both_legs`), not a half-arm. ⚠️ **AN EMPTY `..._ACCOUNTS` ALLOWLIST MEANS *NONE*, DELIBERATELY THE OPPOSITE OF ITS SIBLINGS** — `CONVICTION_SIZING_ACCOUNTS` and `NETTING_ATTRIBUTION_ACCOUNTS` both read empty as ALL, which this file already flags as *"not a safe default, it is the widest one"*; those widen a size and a DB write, this one cancels and re-places a live position's exit, so an unset variable must not arm an order path on every account including real money. Do not "harmonise" it back. `COOLDOWN_S` (default `3600`) and `MAX_ATTEMPTS` (default `3`) bound it per `(account, symbol)`: the condition can hold for DAYS and `modify_protective` is a real cancel-and-re-place, so a per-sweep retry is churn on a live bracket, and a re-assert that keeps failing is a fault whose cause is not the level. An unparseable knob falls back to its **default, not to zero**. At `annotate` (the shipped default) the decision runs in full and writes `runtime_logs/protection_reassert_soak.jsonl` — carrying the effective `mode` beside `apply_scope` (`allowlisted`/`not_allowlisted`) and `acted`, so a held-back row can never read as an applied one — touching no order. Read it at `/api/diag/log_file?name=protection_reassert_soak`. **Tier-2 to flip to `apply`**, and stage it on `ib_paper` first. |
| `PROTECTION_STRAY_GROUP_MODE` | **Clear a trade's OWN prior protection resting under a DIFFERENT group name** (`off` / `annotate` (default) / `apply`; `src/runtime/stray_oca_groups.py` + `ib_client._sweep_stray_oca_groups`, 2026-08-26). `place_protective`'s keyed pre-cancel is scoped **BY NAME** to `oca-protect-t<oca_key>` (`_cancel_oca_group_for_symbol` skips any leg whose `ocaGroup` differs), so when a trade's earlier bracket rests under a legacy `oca-protect-<reqId>` name — or the **bare-numeric** form `834864174` measured live on MGC — that group is never a cancellation candidate and the re-arm mints a SECOND, non-mutually-cancelling group beside it. ⚠️ **THIS IS NOT `BL-20260825-PLACE-PROTECTIVE-COUNTS-THE-CANCEL-CALL-NOT-ITS-EFFECT` AND MUST NOT BE FOLDED INTO IT** — that row is the *symbol-wide fallback* (no `oca_key` → Error 10147 → mint), and all three of its defects are already fixed in `b81458a4`, which is an ancestor of the deployed sha. Captured in the act 2026-08-26T02:08:35Z on `ib_paper`/MHG: a routine trailing amend (`verdict={'sl': 6.31757143}`) armed `oca-protect-t4796` beside `oca-protect-446` + `oca-protect-465` → **300%** at 02:09:36Z, with **no** `no oca_key` warning, **no** 10147 and **no** survivor-join log. ⚠️ **It mints ONCE PER TRADE, at the legacy→keyed transition, so it is FINITE** — every trade opened since the keyed path shipped is keyed from the start; an earlier reading of this as an unbounded generator was wrong. **The rule is *cancel NON-KEYED groups*, never *cancel every other group***: IB nets per contract, so one symbol legitimately hosts one protective group per open trade, and a symbol-wide cancel from one trade's amend would strip a sibling's take-profit (`BL-20260814-IB-PROTECTION-BOOLEAN-NOT-QUANTITY`). A **keyed** group carries its owning trade id in its own name, so a sibling's group is self-identifying and preserved by construction — with **no journal read on the order path**. Five states, never collapsed: `keep_target` · `sibling_keyed` (PRESERVE) · `stray_unkeyed` (the finding) · **`ungrouped` — REPORTED, NEVER CANCELLED** (a leg with no `ocaGroup` cannot be shown to be this trade's abandoned protection and could be a hand-placed exit; the same refusal `attach-ib-target` makes) · `not_protective`. The DECISION is a **pure function** so the policy is arguable in tests rather than against a live position — the lesson of `BL-20260820-OVERCOVER-REMEDIATION-CANCELLED-THE-JOURNAL-MATCHING-LEG`. Runs **only on the keyed path** (an absent `oca_key` is the fallback path and is deliberately not widened from here) and reads **`ib.openTrades()` directly, NOT `self._open_trades`**, because that helper swallows the exception and returns `[]`, collapsing *we could not look* into *nothing rests* (`BL-20260826-OPEN-TRADES-COLLAPSES-A-READ-FAILURE-INTO-AN-EMPTY-BOOK`). A `*_MODE` knob, not a default-off `*_ENABLED` gate, and an unparseable value falls back to **`annotate`** rather than to `off` or `apply` — a typo must not silently switch the observation off, and certainly must not switch a live order path on. **At the shipped `annotate` default the decision runs in full and cancels NOTHING**, so landing it changes no live behaviour. **Tier-2 to flip to `apply`**, and stage it on `ib_paper` via **`PROTECTION_STRAY_GROUP_ACCOUNTS`**. ⚠️ **AN EMPTY ALLOWLIST MEANS *NONE*, deliberately the OPPOSITE of `CONVICTION_SIZING_ACCOUNTS` / `NETTING_ATTRIBUTION_ACCOUNTS`** — those widen a size and a DB write and read empty as ALL, which this file already calls *"not a safe default, it is the widest one"*; this one CANCELS a live position's resting protective legs, so an unset variable must not arm it everywhere. It copies `PROTECTION_REASSERT_ACCOUNTS`'s polarity on purpose; do not "harmonise" it back. **An absent or unnamed `account_id` never cancels** — a caller that cannot name its account cannot arm an order path. ⚠️ **The allowlist scopes the CANCEL, never the MEASUREMENT**: a held-back account is still read and still annotated, so the rows a reviewer needs before widening actually exist — the correction `NETTING_ATTRIBUTION_ACCOUNTS` needed on 2026-08-09, where intersecting the account set at the top of the pass made the account being staged TOWARD invisible. The soak row carries the **effective** `mode` beside `global_mode` and `apply_scope` (`allowlisted` / `not_allowlisted` / `not_apply`), so a held-back row can never read as an applied one. ⚠️ **THAT SENTENCE WAS A PROMISE WITH NO WRITER UNTIL 2026-09-01 — the sweep computed the plan and its one call site DISCARDED it, so there was no soak row to carry anything** (`BL-20260831-STRAY-OCA-SWEEP-ANNOTATE-COMPUTES-A-VERDICT-AND-DISCARDS-IT`, `BL-20260831-STRAY-OCA-APPLY-PATH-HAS-NO-SOAK-SO-ITS-CANCEL-IS-UNPROVABLE`). It is true now: read the rows at `/api/diag/log_file?name=stray_oca_soak` before flipping to `apply` — see that route's row above for what each field does and does not establish. **It exists because the bare global was not stageable**: the two IB accounts are `ib_paper` (`mode: live`, class **paper**) and `ib_live` (`mode: dry_run`, class **real_money**), so a global flip is safe only while `ib_live` happens to be `dry_run` — an argument from current config, not a gate. |
| `IB_BROKER_NAKED_CHECK_SECONDS` | Cadence (seconds, default `300`; `<= 0` disables) of the **IB broker-side naked sweep** (`order_monitor._check_broker_naked_ib_positions`, BL-20260709-IB-BROKER-PROTECTION-UNVERIFIED). The IB analogue of the Alpaca broker-naked sweep: an IB futures/ETF position whose broker OCA bracket was never placed / got cancelled / dropped during a Gateway breaker-flap keeps its journal SL/TP and so is invisible to the DB-driven `_check_naked_positions`, while the monitor may also be blind (the 2026-07-09 MGC incident). Each sweep asks the broker **how much protection actually rests** via `IBClient.protection_coverage` — an **account-wide** `reqAllOpenOrders` read, NOT this client's `openTrades()`, since IB order visibility is per-client-session — and re-arms a GTC OCA via the shared `_attempt_naked_autoprotect` when none does. *(Corrected 2026-08-16: this row named `IBClient.has_protective_orders`, which is a DIFFERENT accessor — a boolean "does any leg rest?". The sweep body calls `protection_coverage` and reads `covered_qty`; field beats comment. The distinction is load-bearing, not pedantic: the boolean answers `True` for a stop-only book, which is exactly the grading that let both live `ib_paper` positions sit target-naked — `BL-20260816-COVERAGE-IS-ONE-SIDED`.)* **Coverage is TWO-SIDED** (2026-08-16): `protection_coverage` returns `stop_qty` and `target_qty` beside the back-compat `covered_qty`, because a stop and a take-profit are **not** interchangeable — a position holding a full stop and no target is stop-covered and still can only stop out or run. The summary counts `target_naked` separately from `broker_naked`, and the target side **alerts without re-arming** (placing a target is decision-time geometry, not a safety re-arm, so it is not done blind on a sweep). ⚠️ **THAT ALERT'S 6h-PER-(account,symbol) COOLDOWN IS DURABLE AS OF 2026-08-23 AND MUST STAY THAT WAY** (`runtime_logs/target_naked_alert_state.json`, wall-clock keyed). It was a module global on `time.monotonic()` — both per-PROCESS — while the condition it rate-limits is broker state that outlives any process, so **every trader restart re-armed it**. Measured over 376 ERROR+ rows (`/api/bot/logs?level=error`; cap 1000, so NOT truncated; oldest 2026-08-17T08:03Z, so 08-16 is unmeasured rather than empty): **202 of them — 53.7% of the entire CRITICAL/ERROR feed over 6.5 days** — were `ib_target_naked` for two `ib_paper` positions in an already-filed state, peaking at 31/symbol on 08-20 against a declared ceiling of 4/day. Mechanism confirmed directly, not inferred: 9 distinct trader process starts on 08-23 (`exit_interval_soak`) vs exactly 9 MES pages (**n=1 day** — the diag log page truncates at 1000 lines). `Level.CRITICAL` is chosen precisely so this reaches Telegram, so the flood trained the operator past the one channel reserved for an unprotected position — the desensitized-alarm P1. **Per-process latching is not always wrong** (`exit_loop_health` breaches alert once per process, correctly, because `max_interval_ms` IS a per-process measurement); it is wrong here, which is why `silent_refusal_alert` / `account_reachability_alert` / `prop_fills_staleness` all persist their latch to `runtime_logs/*_state.json`. An unreadable latch **alerts rather than suppressing** — failing loud is the only safe direction on a safety page, and it makes a permanently-broken latch announce itself as spam instead of as silence (`BL-20260823-TARGET-NAKED-COOLDOWN-RESETS-ON-EVERY-RESTART`). ⚠️ **THE OVER-COVER SIDE NOW PAGES THE OPERATOR, AND UNTIL 2026-08-25 IT DID NOT** (`_emit_stop_over_cover_alert`). The DETECTION has been right since `BL-20260816-IB-STOPS-OVER-COVER-IN-DISJOINT-OCA-GROUPS` — the sweep counts `over_covered` and logs a `logger.error` — but **`logger.error` reaches the systemd journal and nothing else**: it never writes `outcomes.jsonl`, which is what feeds Telegram, the `/api/bot/notifications` banner and `/api/bot/logs?level=error`. Measured 2026-08-25 over a 388-row ERROR+ feed spanning 2026-08-20T07:01Z–2026-08-25T09:28Z: **ZERO** rows mention over-cover, while `/api/diag/ib_open_orders?account_id=ib_paper` read the same session showed `ib_paper` MHG holding a **29-lot position against TWO disjoint OCA groups** (`oca-protect-416`, `oca-protect-432`), each carrying a 29-lot STP **and** a 29-lot LMT — 58 of stop against 29 of position, **200%**. Live, correctly detected, invisible on every surface a human reads. **CRITICAL, because OCA cancels only WITHIN a group**: one stop firing flattens the position and the other group's legs are still resting to sell 29 more into a **naked SHORT**. **Detect-only, deliberately** — it pages and cancels nothing, because the one attempt to remediate this class automatically cancelled the leg that MATCHED the journal (`BL-20260820-OVERCOVER-REMEDIATION-CANCELLED-THE-JOURNAL-MATCHING-LEG`). The WITHIN-one-group case stays a `logger.warning` and does **not** page: a fill cancels its own group's siblings, so it is self-limiting. ⚠️ **THE COOLDOWN IS THE SHARED ONE, NOT A COPY** — `_cooldown_admits(kind, key, cooldown_s)` was generalised out of the target-naked latch in the same change and both callers use it, because copy-pasting a latch is exactly how the per-PROCESS `time.monotonic()` defect that put 202 CRITICALs on the operator's channel would return in the copy. Same durable 6h-per-(account,symbol) shape, state at `runtime_logs/stop_over_cover_alert_state.json`, allowlisted on `/api/diag/log_file?name=stop_over_cover_alert_state` **in the same commit that shipped the writer** (a latch that suppresses a CRITICAL and cannot be inspected is worse than no latch — the `exit_loop_health` #8778 lesson). An unreadable latch **alerts rather than suppressing**. **Cadence-gated (not per-tick)** because the account-wide IB order read must stay clear of the IB tick-latency / pacing wedge class (BL-20260609) — this is the deliberate exception to naked-autoprotect's per-tick baseline (the read is the cost, the re-arm is baseline). Fail-safe: a `None` read (breaker open / gateway wedged / ambiguous) is skipped, never re-armed. Same on-by-default `_SECONDS`-knob shape as `ACCOUNT_REACHABILITY_CHECK_SECONDS`. |

## Diagnostic API (S-051)

Token-gated read-only surface for PM-side Claude / operator scripts. All
endpoints return 503 if `DIAG_READ_TOKEN` is unset, 401 on bad bearer.

| Endpoint | Returns |
|----------|---------|
| `GET /api/diag/snapshot?limit=N` | bundle: heartbeat, status, audit tail, order_packages, trades, vm_health, service states |
| `GET /api/diag/audit?limit=N` | tail of `runtime_logs/signal_audit.jsonl` |
| `GET /api/diag/journal?table={order_packages\|trades}&limit=N` | read-only SELECT |
| `GET /api/diag/audit_query?since=&until=&event=&strategy=&symbol=&side=&limit=&offset=` | **historical, time/event-filtered audit read** backed by the `trade_journal.db::signals` dual-write — reaches arbitrary history (unlike `/audit` + `/log_file?name=audit`, which tail only the last `_MAX_LIMIT`=1000 lines ≈ ~15 min of a busy day). `since`/`until` are ISO-8601 (`Z`/`+00:00`/naive all accepted, treated as UTC); `event` matches the audit event type (`regime_shadow_gate`, `vwap_eval`, …) inside the `meta` JSON; `strategy`/`symbol`/`side` exact-match typed columns; `offset` pages. Rows are newest-first and carry the typed columns merged with the full `meta` payload (`regime`/`adx_14`/`enforced`/`cell`/…). `dual_write_present:false` + `error:signals_table_absent` ⇒ the dual-write hasn't populated `signals` (check `SIGNAL_DUAL_WRITE_DISABLED`). |
| `GET /api/diag/status` | heartbeat + status.json + vm_health |
| `GET /api/diag/services` | `systemctl is-active` per allowlisted unit |
| `GET /api/diag/ib_state` | **IB connection-state legibility** (BL-20260707-IB-STATE-LEGIBILITY). Read-only view of `runtime_logs/ib_state.json` — the per-tick snapshot the TRADER process writes of each live `IBClient`'s connection state (`src.units.accounts.ib_client.write_ib_state_file`, hooked into the `src/main.py` tick beside `run_account_reachability_check`). Answers at a glance the question that kept being confusing: **is IB connected, and is a current failure a TRANSITORY circuit-breaker backoff (`state:"breaker_open"`, `likely_wedged:false` — auto-recovers, normal in IBKR's ~03:45–05:45 UTC reset window) or a REAL wedge (`likely_wedged:true`, `consecutive_failures>=3` — needs a look)?** Per-client fields: `state ∈ {connected, breaker_open, disconnected, never_connected}`, `connected`, `account_data_ready`, `breaker_open`/`breaker_seconds_remaining`, `consecutive_failures`, `likely_wedged`, `last_ok_utc`/`last_ok_age_seconds`, `last_fail_utc`/`last_fail_reason`. Plus (2026-08-22) **`probe_cache_seconds_remaining`** + `probe_cache_configured_s` — whether a cached liveness verdict is currently standing in for a probe and for how much longer (`IB_PROBE_CACHE_S`). ⚠️ **`null` is NOT `0.0`**: `null` means *no trusted verdict* (never probed · cache disabled · a failure cleared it), `0.0` would mean *expiring now* — a reader that cannot tell them apart reads a DISABLED cache as a just-expired one. Without this field a reader cannot distinguish a process skipping probes from one paying 6.5 s for them on every call, which is the entire subject of `BL-20260816-IB-QUEUE-TIMEOUT-EXCEEDS-EXIT-BUDGET`. Envelope: `{present, path, generated_at, age_seconds, clients[]}`. `present:false` until the trader writes the file (it has not dispatched to an IB account since restart); a large `age_seconds` while the trader is ticking means the writer hook isn't running (deploy check). **Pure file read — never opens a socket, never gates a trade.** |
| `GET /api/diag/journalctl?unit=<name>&lines=N&since=<iso>&until=<iso>` | systemd journal tail; `since`/`until` accept strict ISO-8601 (`2026-05-10T21:13:00Z`) and forward to `journalctl --since`/`--until` for historical-window pulls (PR #821, FU-20260511-001) |
| `GET /api/diag/log_file?name={audit\|status\|heartbeat\|bot_log\|advisory_decisions\|shadow_predictions\|shadow_predictions_backfill\|ibkr_mes_pull\|news_decisions\|conviction_sizing\|conviction_arbitration\|exit_ladder_soak\|fc_geometry_soak\|exit_lever_soak\|target_extension_soak\|protection_reassert_soak\|stray_oca_soak\|bybit_coverage_soak\|allocator_soak\|arbitration_fanout_soak\|pairs_soak\|exposure_soak\|netting_attribution_soak\|package_leg_coverage\|exit_loop_health\|exit_interval_soak\|cash_settlement_soak\|work_decision_transit\|work_decision_prompted\|prop_ticket_risk_soak\|account_reachability_alert_state\|trainer_reachability_alert_state\|silent_refusal_alert_state\|prop_fills_staleness_state\|target_naked_alert_state\|stop_over_cover_alert_state\|strategy_builder_exception_alert_state\|exit_loop_health_alert_state\|daily_cap_alert_state\|operator_alerts\|close_wedge_standing\|prop_status_request\|prop_sl_tp_alert\|prop_monitor_pulse\|orphan_events\|conflict_taxonomy_soak\|macro_thesis_soak\|bybit_over_cover_alert_state\|heartbeat_check_state\|liveness_watchdog_state\|pending_pings_delivered}&lines=N` | log file tail. `conviction_sizing` (P2) / `conviction_arbitration` (P3) are the observe-only unified-confidence soak logs — the would-be conviction size / arbitration vs the actual, never order-influencing; tail them to verify the soak is accruing before P4/P5 graduate it. `exit_ladder_soak` is the ExitPlan laddered-vs-single-target soak (dynamic-take-profit consistency P3 — also surfaced publicly at `/api/bot/exit-ladder/soak`). `exit_lever_soak` is the M20 stale-stop ANNOTATE soak (observe-only "the lever would have exited here" rows written pre-YAML-declare by trend_donchian's monitor — the Tier-3 rollout evidence trail). **`target_extension_soak`** is the exit-geometry rebuild's ANNOTATE soak (2026-08-23): `_base.monitor` has declared `{"tp": float}` — move the take-profit — since it was written and **no strategy has ever produced one** (AST-verified), while everything downstream is already live (`interpret_verdict` parses a `tp` delta independently of `sl`, `_apply_update` routes it, `modify_open_order` amends the resting leg). Only the producer was missing. The donchian + pullback monitors now evaluate the extension decision each tick and write a row **instead of** returning a `tp` verdict — a test pins the verdict byte-identical with and without the soak. ⚠️ **Read `expectation_state` BESIDE `extension_state`**: 28 of 52 enabled legs declare `tp_r >= 50` and so have no target to extend FROM, and their rows land `sentinel_no_expectation` / `no_expectation_declared` — a soak read on `extension_state` alone would score that as "the lever never fires" when it means "there was never a target". The thesis is each family's OWN entry condition re-evaluated (donchian: is the channel still being pushed; pullback: does ADX still clear its declared `adx_min`), per `exit-mechanism-construction-PROCESS.md` § 4 — and an unreadable thesis is `thesis_unknown`, which NEVER extends, because "we did not check" must not become "it holds". `not_approaching` is deliberately not logged (the ordinary state of nearly every trade on nearly every tick). `fc_geometry_soak` is the M19 D1 fc-geometry soak (placed SL/TP + decision-time fc snapshot per opening order — also at `/api/bot/fc-geometry/soak`). `allocator_soak` is the M18 portfolio capital-allocator soak (would-pick vs routed + regret on ≥2-candidate ticks — also at `/api/bot/allocator/soak`). **`arbitration_fanout_soak`** is the per-account arbitration soak (2026-08-30, `BL-20260830-TREND-DONCHIAN-SOL-SIGNALS-144-TIMES-AND-JOURNALS-NOTHING-ON-BYBIT-1`): `aggregate_intents` elects ONE winner per SYMBOL **globally, before** the per-account fan-out, so an account running the same symbol as the winner's account is silently STARVED — it produces no order package, which makes it invisible to every per-account detector (that account places fine for its other legs) AND to the journal (no rows to grade). One row per multi-account symbol per tick, carrying **six** never-collapsed states — `routed` / `starved` / **`no_winner`** / **`winner_unattributed`** / `no_candidates` / **`unknown`** (an unreadable roster — *we could not look*, emphatically NOT `no_candidates`). ⚠️ **`starved` MEANS *ANOTHER ACCOUNT TOOK THE WINNER FROM ME*, AND THE ROW SAID SOMETHING WIDER UNTIL 2026-08-30** (`BL-20260830-FANOUT-SOAK-GRADES-A-NO-WINNER-TICK-AS-STARVED`). A tick on which NO strategy won the symbol at all graded every candidate-holding account `starved`, reasoning that *"nothing routed while strategies were asking to, so that IS starvation"* — a real condition, but not the one this soak sizes: there is no other account to have lost to, and the cause is upstream (candidates held, gated or flat) where fanning arbitration out is not the remedy. **Measured on the COMPLETE live file the day the soak shipped** — n=9 rows, 2026-08-30T14:25:15Z→19:03:56Z (`lines=1000` requested, 9 returned), **15 account-gradings**: **13 graded `starved`, of which 11 were no-winner ticks and only 2 were the finding** (`trend_donchian_eth_prop`/`breakout_1` taking ETHUSDT from `bybit_1`, twice) — the headline overstated it **6.5×** in the sole evidence base for the Tier-3 per-account fan-out. All 19 unit tests passed throughout: they asserted the definition as written, not whether the populations were separable. ⚠️ **THE POPULATIONS ARE PUBLISHED SEPARATELY AND MUST NOT BE POOLED** — `starved_count` (the finding) beside `no_winner_count` and `winner_unattributed_count` (a winner resolving to no account: a roster gap, cross-checked by `unattributed_strategies`, never observed live as of 2026-08-30). ⚠️ **A `no_winner` ROW IS STILL WRITTEN, DELIBERATELY** — it is the DENOMINATOR, and dropping it would leave a reader the finding with no way to see how often the symbol had contenders and still routed nothing. ⚠️ **READ `fanout_schema` BEFORE POOLING ROWS**: a row with **no** `fanout_schema` key predates 2026-08-30 and its `starved_accounts` CONFLATES both populations, so mixing it with a `fanout_schema: 2` row re-creates the overstatement in the analysis instead of the code. ⚠️ **A `starved` row still does NOT mean "this account would have traded"**: whether that account's candidate then survives its own regime gate and conflict resolution is **unmeasured here**, and asserting it would be the unprovenanced inference this repo keeps paying for. Read `starved_accounts` beside `accounts_graded`, the `max_multiple`/`measured_n` discipline, and `unattributed_strategies` for a candidate that maps to no account (recorded, never silently dropped). ⚠️ **Read the effective `mode` beside `global_mode` and `apply_scope`** — `ARBITRATION_FANOUT_ACCOUNTS` empty means **NONE** (the `PROTECTION_REASSERT_ACCOUNTS` polarity, deliberately opposite to `CONVICTION_SIZING_ACCOUNTS`), and the allowlist scopes the BINDING never the MEASUREMENT, so a held-back account is still assessed and annotated. ⚠️ **`apply` IS IMPLEMENTED as of 2026-08-31 (Tier-3, operator-approved), and this row said the OPPOSITE until then** — it read *"`apply` is NOT implemented … it refuses back to `annotate` with `apply_implemented: false` on every row"*, which was true of the annotate-only build and became false the moment the apply path landed. **Do not re-quote it.** The row's `apply_implemented` field was hardcoded `false` for that whole period, so a session reading the LOG would have concluded the capability does not exist — the field was present and confidently wrong, which is worse than a missing one. ⚠️ **ARMED ON `bybit_1` 2026-08-31T07:47Z (Tier-3, operator-approved) — and this row said the OPPOSITE until then.** It read *"Live routing is nonetheless UNCHANGED today … the allowlist ships EMPTY, which for this knob means NONE"*, which was true of the shipped default and became false the moment the allowlist was armed. **Do not re-quote it.** Arming is still TWO coordinated settings and BOTH are now set: `ARBITRATION_FANOUT_MODE=apply` **and** `ARBITRATION_FANOUT_ACCOUNTS=bybit_1`, verified on `/proc/<MainPID>/environ` (never the `.env`, which says only what the NEXT restart picks up). Every other account remains at `not_allowlisted`, which for this knob still means NONE. ⚠️ **ARMED, AND NOW EXERCISED AT THE DECISION LEVEL — this paragraph said the OPPOSITE until 2026-08-31T20:31Z and the old text must not be re-quoted.** It read *"as of 2026-08-31T07:51Z the soak held zero rows since the 07:47Z restart … so the fan-out has never elected or routed anything"*, which was a true reading of an 8-hour PRE-DEPLOY quiet and stopped being true at 08:07Z the same morning. Re-measured on the COMPLETE file (35 rows; `lines=1000` requested, 35 returned, so not a truncated tail): **ELEVEN rows carry `mode: apply` · `apply_implemented: true` · `plan_state: planned` · `applied: true`**, spanning 2026-08-31T08:07:07Z (SOLUSDT / `ict_scalp_sol_5m`) to 2026-08-31T20:12:09Z (AVAXUSDT / `avax_pullback_2h`), each with `rounds_applied` naming `bybit_1`. ⚠️ **BUT NO CONTESTED SYMBOL HAS BEEN RESOLVED, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT:** all 11 read `accounts_planned: 1` · `accounts_elected: 1` · `accounts_graded: 1` · `starved_count: 0` on a single account, so the fan-out has only ever run where per-account election is a NO-OP against global arbitration. The defect it exists to fix is an account STARVED by another taking its symbol, and not one applied row has that shape — so this is evidence the code path EXECUTES, never that it routes correctly under contention. Still do not widen the allowlist beyond `bybit_1` until a `trades` row appears on an account the same soak row lists in `rounds_applied[*].accounts`. **What a review session reads to grade it:** `apply_implemented: true` on every row (a `false` means the deployed code predates the fan-out) · `plan_state` ∈ `planned` / `absent` (*we did not look* — the caller passed no plan; **NOT** "it elected nothing") · **`applied` + `rounds_applied`, the EFFECT** — `applied: false` beside a non-empty `rounds_planned` is the STAGED state, the fan-out decided and the allowlist held it back, which `apply_scope` explains · `accounts_elected` beside `accounts_planned`, never a bare count. ⚠️ **A soak row proves the DECISION, never the ORDER** — the end-to-end evidence is a `trades` row on an account the same row lists in `rounds_applied[*].accounts`. Tracked in `OPEN-ITEMS.json` as `OI-20260831-PER-ACCOUNT-ARBITRATION-SHIPPED-NOT-YET-ARMED-OR-EXERCISED` (loud) — ⚠️ whose id still reads *NOT-YET-ARMED* and is **half-stale as of 2026-08-31**: it IS armed, it is not exercised. The id is deliberately not renamed because this file and several backlog rows link it by name; read that row's `summary`, not its id. Shipped in the SAME commit as its writer. **`bybit_coverage_soak`** is the staged Bybit graded-book coverage basis (2026-09-02): one row per (account, symbol) per naked sweep, saying WHICH coverage figure decided that row. ⚠️ **Read `verdicts_differ` — it is the field a Tier-2 reviewer reads before widening `BYBIT_GRADED_COVERAGE_ACCOUNTS` beyond `bybit_1`**: it counts rows where the graded and side-blind figures reached OPPOSITE covered/uncovered verdicts, i.e. where arming would have changed the outcome, and is `null` when only one verdict exists (*we could not compare*, never *they agree*). ⚠️ **A row is written for EVERY Bybit account, not only the allowlisted one** — the allowlist scopes the BINDING, never the MEASUREMENT, or the account being staged TOWARD (`bybit_2`, real money) would be invisible in exactly the rows needed to widen to it. ⚠️ **Read `mode` (effective) beside `global_mode` (requested) and `apply_scope`** (`allowlisted` / `not_allowlisted` / `not_apply`), and `basis` (`graded` / `side_blind`) for which figure actually decided — a held-back row can therefore never read as an applied one. ⚠️ **`graded_qty` is `null`, NEVER `0.0`, when unmeasured**: zero is a real and serious reading (nothing protects this book). ⚠️ **A ROW RECORDS THE COVERAGE DECISION, NOT AN ORDER** — `decision: rearm_indicated` means a hole was measured; whether a top-up or Full-mode re-arm was then attempted, and whether it succeeded, is the sweep's summary and log. ⚠️ **ONE ROW PER (ACCOUNT, SYMBOL) PER SWEEP, not one per journal row** — a netted symbol holds many rows against one exchange position and the post-re-arm cache marker would otherwise be persisted as if it were a venue reading; do not read the row count as a decision count. At `off` it writes nothing. Shipped in the SAME commit as its writer. **`stray_oca_soak`** is the stray-OCA-group sweep's soak (2026-09-01, `BL-20260831-STRAY-OCA-SWEEP-ANNOTATE-COMPUTES-A-VERDICT-AND-DISCARDS-IT` + `BL-20260831-STRAY-OCA-APPLY-PATH-HAS-NO-SOAK-SO-ITS-CANCEL-IS-UNPROVABLE`). `PROTECTION_STRAY_GROUP_MODE`'s row below has promised since 2026-08-26 that a held-back row *"can never read as an applied one"* — while `_sweep_stray_oca_groups` RETURNED a complete plan (mode / global_mode / apply_scope / read_state / stray_groups / preserved_groups / ungrouped_seen) and its ONE call site **discarded the return value**, leaving a `logger.warning` into journald as the only output: rolling, unstructured, and unreachable by `probe_soak.py`. So the gate that CANCELS a live position's protective legs told a Tier-2 reviewer to read rows that did not exist. ⚠️ **`decision` is three states, never collapsed** — `stray_unkeyed` (the finding) · `no_strays` (we read the book and nothing was stray) · `could_not_look` (the `openTrades()` read failed, which is emphatically **NOT** evidence that no strays rest). ⚠️ **A `no_strays` row is written deliberately: it is the DENOMINATOR** — without it *"the sweep is quiet"* and *"the sweep is not running"* render identically, the unstated-denominator error corrected on `arbitration_fanout_soak` two days earlier. ⚠️ **Read `acted` beside `decision`, never `decision` alone**: `acted` is the EFFECT, and a `mode: annotate` / `global_mode: apply` / `apply_scope: not_allowlisted` row carrying the finding is the STAGED state, not a cancel. ⚠️ **`cancel_calls_made` is the count of CANCEL CALLS, NOT an outcome** — it keeps that name on purpose, because renaming it would hide `BL-20260825-PLACE-PROTECTIVE-COUNTS-THE-CANCEL-CALL-NOT-ITS-EFFECT`; branch on `verify_state` and `still_resting`. The apply-side read-back is **ABSENT, not zeroed**, when nothing acted (a zero would assert a verification nobody performed), and `legs_by_state` names all five leg states with explicit zeros so it sums to `legs_seen` checkably rather than by trust. At `off` it writes nothing, keeping that mode byte-for-byte unchanged on disk. Shipped in the SAME commit as its allowlist entry. `pairs_soak` is the M22 D2 market-neutral pairs-sleeve soak (per-pair spread/z decision + placement/close outcome — also at `/api/bot/pairs/soak`). ⚠️ **An `open`/`open_failed` row carries `leg_placement` as of 2026-08-30, and an `open` row WITHOUT that key predates it** — that absence is the version marker, which is why the field is emitted even when every entry is degraded. `exposure_soak` is the gross-exposure observation soak (per-account multiple + venue session per cadence window — also at `/api/bot/exposure/soak`; read `max_multiple` beside `measured_n`). `netting_attribution_soak` is the netting partial-close attribution soak (BL-20260801): one row per journal row the reconciler would reduce/close, with the SELECTION basis (`leg_gone`/`fifo`) and the PRICE provenance (`anchor_status`) as SEPARATE fields — at the default `NETTING_ATTRIBUTION_MODE=annotate` this is the exact row list to review before flipping to `apply`. **`exit_loop_health` is NOT a soak log** — it is the M20 exit-loop liveness state (a single small JSON object, not JSONL), and it is the only surface that can say whether the decoupled exit evaluation is still running. It matters because the decouple took exit evaluation OUT of the liveness watchdog's coverage: that coverage was never a probe, it was the fact that exit evaluation ran INLINE on the tick whose heartbeat the watchdog measures, so a stalled exit loop is a condition nothing else observes. Four states, never collapsed — `unknown` (could not read) / `never_ran` (loop not started, or `EXIT_LOOP_DECOUPLE_DISABLED` set — emphatically NOT healthy) / `fresh` / `stale`; read `max_pass_ms` beside `passes`, since a max over 3 passes is not the claim a max over 3000 is. Added 2026-08-12: #8778 shipped the writer (whose docstring says "for the diag surface") with no allowlist entry, so the state was written and unreadable on the one surface a relay-bound session can reach. **`exit_interval_soak` is its DURABLE half** (added 2026-08-16, operator-directed): every field in `exit_loop_health` lives in a module global that starts empty and is **never reloaded**, so `max_interval_ms` is scoped to ONE process — and the trader redeploys on **every merge to `main`**, measured at **five OBSERVED processes in ~10 h** on 2026-08-16 (counted from `process_started_utc`, NOT from merge times — a merge does not promptly restart the trader, and counting merges over-counted this by one). A max over a short window is systematically **LOW**, so the in-memory grade reads most reassuring exactly when the system is busiest; the only reading that ever approached the 60 s requirement came from the single process that survived a quiet overnight window (n=694, 98.2%). The soak appends **one row per completed pass**, so the max becomes a property of the DATA rather than of a process's lifetime, and `summary.max_interval_ms` is computed over the whole file rather than the returned page. `interval_ms: null` marks the **first pass of a process** — no prior completion to measure from, which is a different fact from an interval of zero and is what keeps a restart gap from reading as a real interval. Read `summary.max_interval_ms` beside `intervals_measured` and `processes_seen`, the `max_multiple`/`measured_n` discipline. **`cash_settlement_soak`** is the T+1 cash-account settlement soak (2026-08-29, `BL-20260823-ALPACA-CASH-ACCOUNT-SETTLEMENT-UNMODELLED`): one row per order-path evaluation on an alpaca account, carrying the settled basis the sizer WOULD have been given. **`would_have_reduced_usd` is the field to read before flipping `ALPACA_CASH_SETTLEMENT_MODE` to `apply`** — it is how much the gate would actually have taken off the basis, i.e. whether it does anything at all. ⚠️ **Read `state` beside the money, never the money alone:** `measured` and `journal_unreadable` can carry the SAME `basis_usd` while meaning opposite things — the second means we could not establish what is unsettled, which is NOT evidence that nothing is. `applied` is the EFFECTIVE outcome and `global_mode` + `apply_scope` say why it differs from what was asked, so a held-back row can never read as an applied one (the distinction `NETTING_ATTRIBUTION_MODE` had to be corrected to make). Shipped in the SAME commit as its writer. **`work_decision_transit`** is Phase H's operator-DECISION transit log — truth IN TRANSIT and nothing else: one row per answer submitted from the SPA that has not yet been committed into the work object in the repo. ⚠️ **READ IT BESIDE THE STORE, NEVER ALONE — a row here is NOT a decision.** `committed` is graded from the `answer` block on the object under `docs/claude/work/objects/`, deliberately, so an answer that never commits leaves its question **UNANSWERED** (transit fails BACK, never forward; a question wrongly shown as answered is a decision nobody made). A row still here whose object carries no answer is an **OPEN WINDOW** — a reportable condition, enumerable with its age on `/api/bot/work/decisions`, not a decision. ⚠️ **It is never pruned**: it is the audit trail of what was submitted and when, and idempotence comes from the repo (the committer sees the `answer` block and skips), not from deleting rows. Allowlisted in the SAME commit that shipped the writer — a committer has to pull this log to close the round-trip, so a transit log with no read surface would mean an answer the operator gave could never become truth. **`work_decision_prompted`** is the ASK half's idempotency marker (2026-09-02): which decision requests the Telegram prompt sweep has already put in front of the operator, so a question is asked ONCE rather than once per cadence. It is on disk and wall-clock keyed rather than a module global, because a per-process marker re-asks every open question on every bot restart — the defect `BL-20260823-TARGET-NAKED-COOLDOWN-RESETS-ON-EVERY-RESTART` records putting 202 CRITICALs on the operator's channel. ⚠️ **READ IT BESIDE THE INBOX, AND READ THE TWO SILENCES APART.** A request absent from `prompted` means the sweep has not asked — which is either *it has not run* or *it HELD*, and those are opposite facts. The sweep holds deliberately when `/api/bot/work/decisions` reports `writeGate.acceptsWrites: false` (a tappable prompt whose taps would 503 is the *reads as dealt with while nothing landed* failure) or when no POLLED bot can carry the buttons; both log a WARNING naming the reason, so `journalctl` is where a hold is distinguished from an outage. ⚠️ An **ABSENT file means the sweep has never prompted anything on this VM**, never that nothing is waiting. Allowlisted in the SAME commit that ships the writer. **`prop_ticket_risk_soak`** is the prop ticket risk-gate soak (2026-08-29): one row per graded ticket, carrying the four never-collapsed states (`within_cushion` / `exceeds_cushion` / `cushion_unknown` / `no_risk_declared`). **It is the evidence trail for the Tier-3 `PROP_TICKET_RISK_GATE_MODE=enforce` flip, and until 2026-08-29 it did not exist** — `src/prop/prop_risk_gate.py`'s MODE block had promised since 2026-08-25 that at `annotate` "a soak row is written" while the module was 318 lines with no file write of any kind, so the decision on an account then **$55.00** from its $4,700 static-DD floor had nothing to be decided on (the 4th recurrence of the `BL-20260825-ALERT-AND-CADENCE-STATE-FILES-SHIP-WITHOUT-A-READ-SURFACE` shape). ⚠️ **Read `state` beside `annotated`**: `annotated` is the EFFECT (did the caveat reach the operator) and `global_mode` the request, so a graded ticket whose caveat never landed cannot read as one that did — the distinction `NETTING_ATTRIBUTION_MODE` had to be corrected to make. ⚠️ **`cushion_usd` is `null`, NEVER `0.0`, when unknown** — zero is a real and *terminal* reading (the account is AT its floor), and *we could not look* is not. **`would_have_capped`** is what `enforce` WOULD have done: it is the count to read before flipping, since at the default `annotate` the suggested SIZE is unchanged. `off` writes nothing, keeping that mode byte-for-byte the pre-2026-08-25 behaviour on disk too. **The five CADENCE + ALERT-LATCH entries added 2026-08-25** (`BL-20260825-ALERT-AND-CADENCE-STATE-FILES-SHIP-WITHOUT-A-READ-SURFACE`) each decide whether an operator gets pinged, or how long a safety input may age, and none of them was readable — the third recurrence of the `exit_loop_health` #8778 shape in two weeks. The failure is always the same: **"the mechanism stopped firing" and "it fired and the condition simply persists" are indistinguishable from outside**, so a broken cadence looks exactly like a quiet one. `exit_loop_health_alert_state` is the latch for the row above it, distinct from the state it grades (a breach alerts once per PROCESS, which is only checkable if the latch can be read). **`strategy_builder_exception_alert_state`** (added 2026-08-25) is the latch that downgrades a REPEATING strategy-builder exception from ERROR to WARN after the first page in a 6h window, keyed on `(strategy, digit-normalised cause)` so a NEW cause on an already-latched strategy still pages immediately. ⚠️ **Read it before concluding a leg has stopped failing** — a WARN-only feed means the latch is holding, not that the fault cleared, and those are opposite facts. It exists because one leg was measured at **240 of 401 rows — 59.9% of the operator ERROR+/CRITICAL feed** (`ict_scalp_mgc_15m: no candle data for symbol=MGC`), burying Bybit venue rejections, over-cover CRITICALs and target-naked pages alike; comparable to the 53.7% `ib_target_naked` flood the durable-cooldown work was done for. ⚠️ **STATE THE POPULATION.** A `limit=400` call returned only **157** rows, over which the same condition reads **131 = 83.4%**; the `limit=1000` call returns **401** rows spanning 2026-08-20T08:16Z→2026-08-25T20:06Z and gives **59.9%**. Both are right for what they cover; 83.4% is NOT the feed-wide figure. ⚠️ **AND THE ATTRIBUTION IS A TRAP — DO NOT READ IT AS ONE BROKEN LEG.** 240 of 240 no-candle rows are `ict_scalp_mgc_15m` and **zero** mention `mgc_trend_1h` at an equal cadence, which looks decisive and is not: `intent_multiplexer` already grades a no-candle exception **WARN**, so a leg hitting the outage is *invisible in an ERROR-level feed*. Its classifier matched `"no candle data returned"` and **2 of 35 builder raise sites — the VARIANT families, `ict_scalp_mgc_15m` among them — omit the word "returned"**, so only they fell through to ERROR (`BL-20260825-TRANSIENT-CLASSIFIER-MISSES-THE-VARIANT-FAMILIES`). The trader journal settles it: at 2026-08-25T20:05:43Z the breaker trips and `get_ohlcv` fails for **MES 1d and MES 15m** in the same seconds with `mes_trend_long_1d` raising. **Fleet-wide outage, one audible family.** The transferable lesson: a DERIVED surface was measured and a conclusion drawn about the underlying condition without first establishing that the surface reports every instance — the feed's own filter was the variable. Zero rows meant zero rows *passed the filter*, never zero events. `daily_cap_alert_state` is the daily-cap latch. **`operator_alerts`** (added 2026-09-01, `BL-20260901-OPERATOR-ALERTS-HAS-NO-READ-SURFACE`) is the durable ring behind the `/api/bot/notifications` banner feed: `execution_diagnostics._append_operator_alert` writes every alert that reaches it — `close_failure`, `orphan_created`, `stuck_package_sweep`, `naked_position`, `monitor_blindness` and nine siblings — as one `{ts, kind, priority, body}` row. ⚠️ **It is the ONLY surface from which a page RATE is recoverable**, which is the desensitised-alarm question this file calls a P1: `/api/bot/notifications` renders the CURRENT banner and nothing else, and these alerts deliberately do **not** ride `outcomes.jsonl`, so `/api/bot/logs?level=error` returns **zero** of them — a silence that reads exactly like *"it never fired"*. Measured 2026-09-01: a live, hours-long `close_failure` on `alpaca_paper`/GLD was rendering on the banner while the full 1000-row ERROR/WARN feed held zero matching rows. ⚠️ **It is NOT a fixed 300-row window — state the population before quoting a rate.** `_OPERATOR_ALERTS_KEEP` is 300 but the trim only fires past **2×** that, so the file holds anywhere from 300 to 600 rows and its oldest row's age is not a constant; read the `ts` span you actually got rather than assuming the window. ⚠️ **A `close_failure` ROW NOW CARRIES `route`, AND A ROW WITHOUT THAT KEY PREDATES 2026-09-02** — that absence is the version marker, the `fanout_schema` discipline. `route` is `page` (a Telegram ping was queued) or `digest` (**no ping was queued**; the condition is carried in the rolled-up digest instead). ⚠️ **DO NOT COMPUTE A PAGE RATE OVER `kind == "close_failure"` WITHOUT SPLITTING ON `route`** — the two populations are different events and pooling them re-creates the overstatement in the analysis rather than the code, exactly as `arbitration_fanout_soak`'s `starved`/`no_winner` pooling did. The row is written on BOTH routes deliberately: suppressing it alongside the ping would make *"we downgraded it"* and *"it never fired"* render identically **here**, on the one surface that can tell them apart. Read `share_hold` and `wedge_transition` beside `route` — they say WHY it was downgraded and which transition it was on. **`close_wedge_standing`** (added 2026-09-02, MI-34) is the durable set of close failures that route `digest`: a single small JSON OBJECT, not JSONL (the `exit_loop_health` shape), because it answers *what is standing right now*. ⚠️ **READ `readState` BEFORE `wedges`** — an ABSENT file on the trader means no wedge has ever been recorded, while an absent file anywhere else means it was never fetched, and only one of those is good news. ⚠️ **THE PAGER IS FLOORED, NOT SILENCED**: a standing wedge still pages once per `CLOSE_WEDGE_REPAGE_HOURS` (default 24h), down from the close-failure backoff's hourly cap. That floor is NOT redundancy — the digest runs on a GitHub runner and `runtime_logs/` is `.gitignore`d and VM-local, so it can only see this ledger through a diag fetch on a scheduled cadence this repo has measured as **late and erratic** — `probes.yml`'s first-ever scheduled run (#34, success, 2026-09-01T10:12:17Z) came **~4h50m late** against its `20 5 * * *` cron, once rather than daily. Do not remove the floor until a digest run is OBSERVED carrying a standing wedge; merged and green are not observations. The three prop files are the only evidence the bot is still doing its half of a MANUAL bridge: **`prop_status_request`** records when each account was last asked for a balance — **read it beside `/api/bot/prop/status`**, because a `stale` freshness with a RECENT ask means the operator has not answered (normal) while a stale or absent ask means the bot stopped asking, which is a defect in the input to `prop_balance`'s sizing-freshness gate AND to the rule-distance panel; measured 2026-08-25 the status was **47.4h old against a 24h threshold** and those two cases could not be told apart from any surface. **`prop_sl_tp_alert`** records which positions have been announced as having crossed a bracket, and is a direct INPUT to `prop_fills_staleness`'s crossing detector — whose `unknown` state means precisely "no entry here", so without it a reader grading that detector cannot see its denominator. **`prop_monitor_pulse`** records when the consolidated monitoring heartbeat last went out. **`pending_pings_delivered`** is the ping DELIVERY ledger — one sha256 per `docs/claude/pending-pings.jsonl` line that `scripts/notify_on_pull.py` has enqueued to the Telegram bridge, and the ONLY surface that tells a delivered operator ping apart from one the drain never ran for (a silent channel and a quiet-but-working one are otherwise identical from outside). To check a specific ping, sha256 the **raw stripped jsonl line** — that is what `_line_hash` hashes, not the parsed payload — and look for the digest here. ⚠️ It is `.gitignore`d and VM-local, and it is anchored to the REPO path (`notify_on_pull.py` hardcodes `REPO_ROOT/runtime_logs`), not to `DATA_DIR`. **Absent means the drain has never enqueued anything on this VM, NOT that nothing was delivered** — a re-provision or re-clone resets it while the pings it recorded were still sent. A hash present here was enqueued; a hash missing here proves nothing. |
| `GET /api/diag/db_info` | resolved `trade_journal.db` path + inode + table list + per-table row counts (and per-table error string when a SELECT raises). Companion to `/journal`; a trader-vs-web-api inode mismatch on the same logical path is the canonical signature of the 2026-05-09 `order_packages returns []` mystery. |
| `GET /api/diag/version` | `{git_sha, git_sha_on_disk, restart_pending, captured_at}` — **`git_sha` is the sha the RUNNING process was loaded from**, captured once at import; `git_sha_on_disk` is the working tree resolved live; `restart_pending` is `git_sha != git_sha_on_disk`, i.e. code was pulled and nothing restarted. ⚠️ **`restart_pending` is `null`, never `false`, when either sha is `unknown`** — *we could not look* is not *they agree*. Used by `scripts/deploy_pull_restart.sh` to confirm a post-deploy restart actually rolled the code forward; `git_sha:"unknown"` on sandbox/dev hosts is a soft failure. ⚠️ **Until 2026-08-23 this endpoint reported the DISK sha and the deploy assertion was VACUOUS** (`BL-20260823-DIAG-VERSION-REPORTS-DISK-SHA-NOT-RUNNING-CODE`): `_resolve_git_sha()` shells `git rev-parse --short HEAD` at REQUEST time, so a `git pull` advanced the reported sha without restarting anything — the endpoint reported the new sha while the old code served, which **is** the 2026-05-09 24h-stale-code state it was built to catch. The deploy script compared it against its own `git rev-parse --short HEAD` over the **same tree** — `X == X`, a check that could not fail. Measured: `/api/diag/version` returned `fced7279` while the same process returned **HTTP 400** for a `log_file` name that exists in `fced7279` (control: a name allowlisted earlier returned 200). Disk had moved; the process had not. |
| `GET /api/diag/shadow_stats?model_id=X&stage=X&since=ISO` | token-gated diag mirror of `GET /api/bot/shadow/stats` (which is not under `/api/diag/`, so the relay can't reach it) — per-`(model_id, stage)` aggregate shadow-prediction stats `{log_present, log_path, records[], count}` (FU-20260516-001). 503 if `ml.shadow.inspector` import fails. |
| `GET /api/diag/exchange_positions?account_id=X` | read-only **exchange-side** open positions per account — the BROKER's truth, not the journal (added 2026-06-19). Per-account `positions` is `null` (could-not-read), `[]` (genuinely flat), or `[{symbol, side, size, entry_price, unrealised_pnl}, …]`. Opens a brief read-only client per account via `account_open_positions` (the same primitive the live reconciler calls); places NO order. Lets a web/PM session confirm whether a journal orphan actually exists on the broker before cleanup. |
| `GET /api/diag/ib_open_orders?account_id=X` | `{captured_at, requested_account_id, count, accounts:[{account_id, exchange, mode, read_state, orders, count, error}]}` — the resting **IB orders** the broker actually holds, per account (added 2026-08-16, closes `BL-20260814-NO-IB-OPEN-ORDERS-READ-SURFACE`). **Every prior consumer of IB order state REDUCES it before anyone sees it** — `IBClient.has_protective_orders` → a boolean, `protection_coverage` → a covered quantity — and neither can be contradicted from outside. That is why the stripped MGC take-profit sat undetected for seven days: the coverage read said "covered" and no session could ask *which orders exist*. This route reduces nothing; the rows are the substrate those verdicts get CHECKED against. Per-account `orders` is **three-state, never collapsed**, with `read_state` naming which so a consumer never infers it from a null: `not_ib` (no such surface — we did not fail, there is nothing to read) · `could_not_look` (`orders: null` — gateway unreachable, breaker open, `ib_port` unset, or a dry/shelved account we deliberately never dial) · `orders_read` (the read SUCCEEDED). ⚠️ **`orders_read` is NOT a confirmed clean read and `[]` does NOT mean the account holds nothing** — corrected 2026-08-26, and this row said the opposite until then. `ib_insync`'s `openTrades()` reads an ACCUMULATED cache and `reqAllOpenOrders` only ADDS to it, while cancel callbacks reach the SUBMITTING client only — so a read-only client never learns of a cancel it did not issue and its view grows monotonically stale. Measured: the route and a fresh client differed by EXACTLY a cancelled pair while agreeing on all six other orders. The envelope now ships a `stale_read_caveat` saying so. Verify a specific order against a FRESH client before acting on its absence or presence (`BL-20260826-DIAG-IB-OPEN-ORDERS-SERVES-A-STALE-MONOTONIC-ORDER-VIEW`). The ENFORCING path is unaffected — the naked sweep uses the trader's own client. **`count` is `null`, never `0`, when we could not look** — a `0` there is exactly the collapse this exists to prevent. Rows carry `symbol`/`local_symbol`/`sec_type`/`exchange`, `order_id`/`perm_id`, `order_type`, `action`, `total_quantity`, `aux_price`/`lmt_price`, `oca_group`, `tif`, `parent_id`, `status`, `filled`/`remaining`. Reads **account-wide** via `reqAllOpenOrders` (IB order visibility is per-client-session, so a readonly client's own `openTrades()` would miss the trader's brackets entirely); opens a brief read-only client per account and places NO order; offloaded to the single-worker account-read executor (BL-20260706-IBCONCURRENCY). Tier 1 | `src.units.accounts.clients::account_ib_open_orders` → `IBClient.list_open_orders` (`src/web/api/routers/diag.py`) |
| `GET /api/diag/bybit_open_orders?account_id=X` | `{captured_at, requested_account_id, count, accounts:[{account_id, exchange, mode, account_class, read_state, result, position_count, order_count, error}]}` — the resting **Bybit orders + POSITION-LEVEL protection**, per account (added 2026-08-22, closes `BL-20260820-PROTECTION-COVERAGE-IS-PRICE-BLIND` criterion 5). The `ib_open_orders` sibling, built for the same reason one level up: every consumer of Bybit protection state REDUCES it first. `order_monitor._bybit_position_protection` returns a covered QUANTITY, and its Full-mode branch returns `covered_qty == size` on any `pos["stopLoss"]` that is merely **non-empty and not `"0"`** — the *string* is the whole test, the PRICE is never read, so a stop anywhere at all grades fully covered. That row was written about IB and explicitly left Bybit unchecked; it is checked now, and ⚠️ **the blast radius is LARGER than the row's** — the IB instance is `ib_paper`, whereas **`bybit_2` is mainnet**. ⚠️ **BOTH collections ARE the protection; reading one is reading half.** Under `BYBIT_TPSL_MODE=full` there is **no resting order** — the stop lives on the POSITION row as `stopLoss`. A surface dumping only open orders would report zero legs for a correctly-protected position and a consumer would grade it **naked**, driving a re-arm on a position that is already protected: the inverse of the bug being chased, and worse. So `result.positions[]` carries the Full-mode `stop_loss`/`take_profit`/`tpsl_mode` and `result.orders[]` the Partial-mode legs with `trigger_price`/`price`, `stop_order_type` and `order_filter`. Both `StopOrder` **and** `Order` filters are read — a resting limit take-profit is an `Order` and is invisible to the `StopOrder` filter, so reading one under-reports target protection. Per-account `result` is **three-state, never collapsed**, with `read_state` naming which: `not_bybit` (nothing to read — not a failure) · `could_not_look` (`result: null` — creds missing, a spot account with no derivative position to protect, or an SDK error) · `orders_read` (a confirmed clean read; empty lists genuinely mean nothing rests). **`position_count`/`order_count` are `null`, never `0`, when we could not look.** ⚠️ **An unset venue price is `null`, never `0.0`** — Bybit reports no-stop as `""` or `"0"`, and a zero would compare against a declared level as a catastrophic divergence when the truth is that no stop is set. **Each position row also carries `position_idx`** (added 2026-08-26) — THE VENUE'S POSITION MODE: `0` = one-way netting, `1` = hedge-long, `2` = hedge-short. Bybit returns it on every position row and the extraction dropped it, so *"is this account one-way or hedge?"* had **no read surface anywhere** and was answerable only from prose — while that question gates a Tier-3 change (arming `BYBIT_HEDGE_MODE_SYMBOLS` flips a live-order setting that could otherwise be neither confirmed beforehand nor verified after). ⚠️ **`null` means the venue did not report it — WE DID NOT LOOK, and it must never be read as 0/one-way**: defaulting an unread mode to the netting value is precisely the reading that would make a hedge account look safe to treat as netted. ⚠️ **PARTIAL BY CONSTRUCTION** — both read paths skip `size <= 0`, so the mode is reported only for symbols that HOLD a position; a FLAT symbol is absent entirely, which is why this does not settle the question for a symbol you are about to switch (`BL-20260826-BYBIT-POSITION-MODE-UNREADABLE-FOR-A-FLAT-SYMBOL`). Both rows are built by ONE `_bybit_position_row` builder so the settleCoin page and the per-symbol cross-check cannot drift in their field set. Inherits the `BL-20260713-BYBIT2-BTC-SETTLECOIN-BLIND` cross-check on **both** halves: a `settleCoin=USDT` page can silently OMIT a live symbol, so it is re-read symbol-scoped and the recovered rows are flagged `settlecoin_blind`. ⚠️ **Until 2026-08-26 that applied to the POSITIONS read ONLY, and this row described it in a way that read as covering the route** — so the surface answered the position question completely and the PROTECTION question partially, which is the worse half to under-report because a missing leg reads as missing protection. Measured on `bybit_1`/ETHUSDT 2026-08-26: the settleCoin ORDER page returned **7 SL legs where the trader's own symbol-scoped read saw 9**, and the resulting leg table was used to reason about a live over-cover. The two halves take **different denominators, deliberately**: positions cross-checks every **configured** symbol the page missed (we do not yet know which symbols hold a position — that is the question); orders cross-checks only the symbols that **hold a position**, because protection is a property of a position and widening it would spend a broker call per instrument on rows nobody grades. ⚠️ **Read `order_symbols_unchecked` before trusting a short `orders` list** — it names the exact `(symbol, filter)` pairs the cross-check could NOT look at, so *we did not look* stays distinct from *nothing rests*; `order_symbols_cross_checked` is the positive denominator. Dedup is by **order id**, not by symbol, since a symbol present on the page can still be under-reported by it. Grades nothing, re-arms nothing, opens no order path, cannot refuse a trade. Whether `_bybit_position_protection` itself should carry the price axis is that row's criterion 4 — a Tier-2/3 change to the ENFORCING path, deliberately **not** proposed here.
